Use a local polynomial estimator to fit the potential outcome functions: Take

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(1,x)

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\gamma}_{0}+\widehat{\gamma}_{1}x,

\displaystyle(\widehat{\gamma}_{0},\widehat{\gamma}_{1})

\displaystyle:=\argmin_{\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(Y_{i}-\gamma_{0}-\gamma_{1}\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)^{2}K_{h}\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)\mathbbm{1}(T_{i}=1),

where
K_{h}(\cdot)=h^{-1}K(\cdot/h)
where
K
is a kernel function,
h
is the optimal bandwidth. Then
\widehat{f}(1,0)=f(1,0)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\partial_{2}\widehat{f}(1,0)=\partial_{2}f(1,0)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, the same for control group. Moreover, the rate of convergence can be made not depending on
\beta
.

SA-6
Additional Distributional Results

This section presents the additional distributional results in the appendix. We continue to use the notations defined at the beginning of Section
SA-4
.

SA-6.1
Low Temperature Treatment Assignment

Recall we consider a conditional estimand given by

\displaystyle\tau_{n,\ell}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}(1;\mathbf{T}_{-i})-Y_{i}(0;\mathbf{T}_{-i})|f_{i}(\cdot),\mathbf{E},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell],\qquad\ell\in\{-,+\},

where
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\operatorname{sgn}(2n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}-1)
. Let
\pi_{*}
be the positive root of
x=\tanh(\beta x)
, and take
\pi_{+}=1/2+\pi_{*}/2
,
\pi_{-}=1/2-\pi_{*}/2
.

Lemma SA-20
.

Suppose Assumptions
SA-1
, and Assumptions 2, and 3 from the main paper hold with
\beta>1
and
h=0
. Define

\displaystyle R_{i,\ell}=\frac{g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})}{1+\pi_{\ell}}+\frac{g_{i}(-1,\pi_{\ell})}{1-\pi_{\ell}},\quad Q_{i,\ell}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{G(U_{i},U_{j})}{\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}(g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})-g_{j}^{\prime}(-1,\pi_{\ell}))\Big|U_{i}\Big],\quad\ell\in\{-,+\}.

Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\max_{\ell\in\{-,+\}}\big|\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n,\ell}\leq t|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell)-\mathbb{P}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(R_{i,\ell}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[R_{i,\ell}]+Q_{i,\ell})(W_{i}-\pi_{\ell})\leq t)\big|

\displaystyle=O\Big(\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}+\sqrt{n\log n}(n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{p+1}{2}}\Big).

Lemma SA-21
.

Suppose Assumptions
SA-1
, and Assumptions 2, and 3 from the main paper hold with
\beta>1
and
h=0
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\max_{\ell\in\{-,+\}}\big|\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n,\ell}\leq t|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell)-L_{n}(t;\beta,\kappa_{1,\ell},\kappa_{2,\ell})\big|=O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{n\log n}{(n\rho_{n})^{p+1}}}+\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}\Big),

where with
\mathsf{Z}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,1)
,

\displaystyle L_{n}(t;\beta,\kappa_{1,\ell},\kappa_{2,\ell})=\mathbb{P}\Big\{n^{-1/2}\Big(\kappa_{2,\ell}(1-\pi_{*}^{2})+\kappa_{1,\ell}^{2}\frac{\beta(1-\pi_{*}^{2})}{1-\beta(1-\pi_{*}^{2})}\Big)^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}\leq t\Big\},

where
\kappa_{s,\ell}=\mathbb{E}[(R_{i,\ell}-\mathbb{E}[R_{i,\ell}]+Q_{i,\ell})^{s}]
for
s=1,2
and
\ell=-,+
.

SA-6.2
Asymmetric Treatment Assignment

Recall the following treatment assignment model from Section A.1: For
\beta\in[0,\infty)
and
h\neq 0
, the treatment vector
\mathbf{T}=(T_{1},\cdots,T_{n})
satisfies a distribution on
\{0,1\}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}(\mathbf{T}=\mathbf{t})\propto\exp\bigg(\frac{\beta}{n}\sum_{i<j}(2t_{i}-1)(2t_{j}-1)+h\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2t_{i}-1)\bigg),\qquad\mathbf{t}\in\{0,1\}^{n}.

Let
\pi
be the unique solution to
x=\tanh(\beta x+h)
.

Lemma SA-22
.

Suppose Assumptions
SA-1
, and Assumptions 2, and 3 from the main paper hold with
\beta>0
and
h\neq 0
. Define

\displaystyle R_{i}=\frac{g_{i}(1,\pi)}{1+\pi}+\frac{g_{i}(-1,\pi)}{1-\pi},\qquad Q_{i}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{G(U_{i},U_{j})}{\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}(g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-g_{j}^{\prime}(-1,\pi))\Big|U_{i}\Big].

Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(R_{i}-\mathbb{E}[R_{i}]+Q_{i})(W_{i}-\pi)\leq t)\big|=O\Big(\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}+\sqrt{n\log n}(n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{p+1}{2}}\Big).

Lemma SA-23
.

Suppose Assumptions
SA-1
, and Assumptions 2, and 3 from the main paper hold with
\beta>0
and
h\neq 0
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}[\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}\leq t]-L_{n}(t;\beta,h,\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\big|=O\Big(\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}+\sqrt{n\log n}(n\rho_{n})^{-(p+1)/2}\Big),

where
L_{n}(\cdot;\beta,h,\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})
is as follows:

\displaystyle L_{n}(t;\beta,h,\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}\Big[n^{-1/2}\Big(\kappa_{2}(1-\pi^{2})+\kappa_{1}^{2}\frac{\beta(1-\pi^{2})^{2}}{1-\beta(1-\pi^{2})}\Big)^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}\leq t\Big]

with
\mathsf{Z}\thicksim\mathsf{N}(0,1)
, and
\kappa_{s}=\mathbb{E}[(R_{i}-\mathbb{E}[R_{i}]+Q_{i})^{s}]
for
s=1,2
.

SA-6.3
Ising Block Treatment Assignment

Recall our notations: For block
k
with
h_{k}\neq 0
or
h_{k}=0,0\leq\beta_{k}\leq 1
,
\pi_{k}
denotes the unique solution to
x=\tanh(\beta_{k}x+h_{k})
. For block
k
with
h_{k}=0,\beta_{k}>1
,
\pi_{k,+}
and
\pi_{k,-}
denote the unique positive and negative solutions to
x=\tanh(\beta_{k}x+h_{k})
, respectively.

Due to the potential existence of low temperature blocks, we use
\bm{sgn}
to collect the average spins in all low temperature blocks, and fill in the positions for high and critical temperature blocks with zeros, that is,

\displaystyle\bm{sgn}=(\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}_{1})\mathbbm{1}(1\in\mathscr{L}),\cdots,\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}_{K})\mathbbm{1}(K\in\mathscr{L})).

And we use
\mathscr{S}
to denote the collection of all possible configurations of
\bm{sgn}
, that is,

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}=\{(s_{k})_{1\leq k\leq K}:s_{k}=-\text{ or }+\text{ if }k\in\mathscr{L},s_{k}=0\text{ otherwise}\}.

Also we denote the conditional fixed point based on
\bm{sgn}=\mathbf{s}
by

\displaystyle\pi_{k,(\mathbf{s})}=\begin{cases}\pi_{k},&\text{ if }k\in\mathscr{H}\cup\mathscr{C},\\
\pi_{k,s_{k}},&\text{ if }k\in\mathscr{L},\end{cases}\qquad 1\leq k\leq K,\mathbf{s}\in\mathscr{S}.

We denote by
\mathscr{R}
the collection of all hyperrectangles in
\mathbb{R}^{K}
.

Lemma SA-24
.

Suppose Assumptions 2, 3, and 6 from the main paper hold. Condition on
\bm{sgn}=\mathbf{s}
,

\displaystyle\bigg\lVert\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n}-\bm{\tau}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}(W_{i}-\pi_{l,(\mathbf{s})})\bigg\rVert_{2}=O_{\psi_{1},tc}(\mathtt{r}_{n}).

where
\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}=(S_{1,l,i,(\mathbf{s})},\cdots,S_{K,l,i,(\mathbf{s})})^{\mathbf{T}}
, where

\displaystyle S_{k,l,i,(\mathbf{s})}=Q_{i,(\mathbf{s})}+\mathbbm{1}(k=l)p_{k}^{-1}(R_{i,l,(\mathbf{s})}-\mathbb{E}[R_{i,l,(\mathbf{s})}]),\qquad 1\leq k,l\leq K,1\leq i\leq n,

with
\overline{\pi}_{(\mathbf{s})}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}\pi_{k,(\mathbf{s})}
,

\displaystyle R_{i,l,(\mathbf{s})}

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{i}(1,\overline{\pi}_{(\mathbf{s})})}{1+\pi_{l,(\mathbf{s})}}+\frac{g_{i}(-1,\overline{\pi}_{(\mathbf{s})})}{1-\pi_{l,(\mathbf{s})}},

\displaystyle Q_{i,(\mathbf{s})}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{G(U_{i},U_{j})}{\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}(g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\overline{\pi}_{(\mathbf{s})})-g_{j}^{\prime}(-1,\overline{\pi}_{(\mathbf{s})}))\Big|U_{i}\Big].

and
\mathtt{r}_{n}=\sqrt{\log n}\max_{1\leq k\leq K}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}(n\rho_{n})^{-1/2}+(n\rho_{n})^{-(p+1)/2}
.

Lemma SA-25
.

Suppose Assumptions 2, 3, and 6 from the main paper hold. Condition on
\bm{sgn}=\mathbf{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathscr{S}}\sup_{A\in\mathscr{R}}

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\beta},\bm{h}}(\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n}-\bm{\tau}_{n}\in A|\bm{sgn}=\mathbf{s})-

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(n^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{(\mathbf{s})}^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{K}+n^{-1/2}\sum_{k\in\mathscr{H}\cup\mathscr{L}}p_{k}\sigma_{k,(\mathbf{s})}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{k,i,(\mathbf{s})}]\mathsf{Z}_{(k)}+n^{-1/4}\sum_{k\in\mathscr{C}}p_{k}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{k,i,(\mathbf{s})}]\mathsf{R}_{(k)}\in A)|

\displaystyle\qquad\quad=O(n^{1/2}\mathtt{r}_{n}+(\log n)^{7/6}n^{-1/6}),

where
\mathsf{Z}_{K}\sim\mathsf{N}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{K\times K})
,
\mathsf{Z}_{(k)}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,1)
for
k\in\mathscr{H}\cup\mathscr{L}
, and
\mathsf{R}_{(k)}
has cummulative distribution function
F_{0}(t)=\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{t}\exp(-z^{4}/12)dz}{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp(-z^{4}/12)dz},t\in\mathbb{R},
for
k\in\mathscr{C}
, with
\mathsf{Z}_{K}
,
\mathsf{Z}_{(k)},k\in\mathscr{H}\cup\mathscr{L}
and
\mathsf{R}_{(k)},k\in\mathscr{C}
mutually independent, and

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{(\mathbf{s})}=(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{k,i,(\mathbf{s})}\mathbf{S}_{k,i,(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}](1-\pi_{k,(\mathbf{s})}^{2})p_{k}^{2})^{1/2}.

Remark SA-3
.

If there is no low temperature block, then
\bm{sgn}=(0,\cdots,0)
almost surely, and
\mathsf{S}
is the singleton set containing
(0,\cdots,0)
. Hence the result reduces to the unconditional distributional approximation.

SA-7
Proofs: Main Paper

SA-7.1
Proof of Theorem 3.1

The conclusion follows from the stochastic linearization result in Lemma
SA-16
, and the Berry-Esseen result for Curie-Weiss magnetization with independent multipliers in Lemma
SA-3
(1) and (2).

SA-7.2
Proof of Theorem 3.2

The conclusion for Hajek estimator follows from the stochastic linearization result in Lemma
SA-16
, and the (uniform in
\beta
) Berry-Esseen result for Curie-Weiss magnetization with independent multipliers in Lemma
SA-4
(1).

The conclusion for MPLE follows from Lemma
SA-9
.

SA-7.3
Proof of Lemma 3.1

The conclusion follows from Lemma
SA-3
and Lemma
SA-4
.

SA-7.4
Proof of Theorem 4.1

The uniform approximation for
\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\beta}_{n}-1)
established in Lemma
SA-9
implies

\inf_{\beta}\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\beta\in\pazocal{I}(\alpha_{1}))\geq\inf_{\beta}\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\sqrt{n}(1-\beta)\geq\mathscr{q})\geq 1-\alpha_{1}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

where
\mathscr{q}
is the
\alpha_{1}
quantile of
\min\{\max\{\mathsf{T}_{c_{\beta,n},n}^{-2}-\mathsf{T}_{c_{\beta,n},n}^{2}/(3n),0\},1\}
.

Then by a Bonferroni correction argument, the second step coverage can be lower bounded by

\displaystyle\inf_{\beta\in[0,1]}\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\tau_{n}\in\widehat{\pazocal{C}}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}))

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\beta\in[0,1]}\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\tau_{n}\in\widehat{\pazocal{C}}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}),\beta\in\pazocal{I}(\alpha_{1}))-\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\beta\notin\pazocal{I}(\alpha_{1})).

Observe that the event
\tau_{n}\in\widehat{\pazocal{C}}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
conincides with the event
\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}\in[\mathtt{L},\mathtt{U}]
, where
\mathtt{U}=\sup_{\beta\in\pazocal{I}(\alpha_{1})}H_{n}(1-\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2};K_{n},K_{n},c_{\beta,n})
,
\mathtt{L}=\inf_{\beta\in\pazocal{I}(\alpha_{1})}H_{n}(\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2};K_{n},K_{n},c_{\beta,n})
.
Hence

\displaystyle\inf_{\beta\in[0,1]}\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\tau_{n}\in\widehat{\pazocal{C}}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}),\beta\in\pazocal{I}(\alpha_{1}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{\beta\in[0,1]}\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}\in[H_{n}(\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2};K_{n},K_{n},c_{\beta,n}),H_{n}(1-\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2};K_{n},K_{n},c_{\beta,n})],\beta\in\pazocal{I}(\alpha_{1}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{\beta\in[0,1]}\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}\in[H_{n}(\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2};K_{n},K_{n},c_{\beta,n}),H_{n}(1-\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2};K_{n},K_{n},c_{\beta,n})])-\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(\beta\in\pazocal{I}(\alpha_{1})).

Theorem 2 shows that the quantiles of the distributions of
\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}
can be uniformly approximated by quantiles from
H_{n}(\cdot;\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},c_{\beta,n})
, if
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
are correctly specified, and the confidence interval is conservative, if we use upper bound
\mathtt{K}_{n}
for
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
. The conclusion then follows.

SA-7.5
Proof of Theorem 4.2

The conclusion follows from Theorem 4.1 and Lemma
SA-18
.

SA-7.6
Proof of Lemma 5.1

The conclusion follows from Lemma
SA-21
.

SA-7.7
Proof of Lemma 5.2

The conclusion follows from Lemma
SA-23
.

SA-7.8
Proof of Lemma 5.3

The conclusion follows from Lemma
SA-25
.

SA-8
Proofs: Section
SA-2

SA-8.1
Proof of Lemma
SA-1

Using Gaussian integral identity
\exp(v^{2}/2)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp\left(-u^{2}/2+uv\right)du
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{w}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\exp\left(\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}u+h\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\right)\right)}{2^{n}\exp\left(n\log\cosh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}u+h\right)\right)}f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}}(u)du.

SA-8.2
Proof of Lemma
SA-2

Our proof is divided according to the different temperature regimes.

The High Temperature Regime.

We introduce the handy notation given by
F(v):=-\frac{1}{2}v^{2}+\log\cosh(\sqrt{\beta}v+h)
.
For the high temperature regime, we note that the term in the exponential can be expanded across its global minimum
v^{*}
(which satisfies the first order stationary point condition given by
v^{*}=\sqrt{\beta}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}v^{*}+h)
) by

\displaystyle F(v)

\displaystyle=F(v^{*})+F^{\prime}(v^{*})(v-v^{*})+\frac{1}{2}F^{(2)}(v^{*})(v-v^{*})^{2}+O((v-v^{*})^{3})

\displaystyle=F(v^{*})-\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v^{*}+h))(v-v^{*})^{2}+O((v-v^{*})^{3}).

Therefore, to obtain the limit of the expectation, we note that by the Laplace method given similar to the proof of Lemma
SA-3
and the definition of
\mathsf{V}_{n}:=n^{-1/2}\mathsf{U}_{n}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}]=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}v\exp\left(-nF(v)\right)dv}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp(-nF(v))dv}=v^{*}(1+O(n^{-1})).

Then, we note that for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, when
h=0
and
\beta<1
we use the Laplace method again to obtain that for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}])^{2\ell}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}(v-v^{*})^{2\ell}\exp(-n(F(v)-F(v^{*})))dv}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp(-n(F(v)-F(v^{*})))dv}(1+O(n^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}{\bigg(}\frac{2}{n(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v^{*}+h))}{\bigg)}^{\ell}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{2\ell+1}{2}{\bigg)}(1+O(n^{-1})).

Then we can obtain that for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(t(\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}]))]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{\ell}}{\ell!}\mathbb{E}[(\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}])^{\ell}]=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{2\ell}}{(2\ell)!}\mathbb{E}[(\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}])^{2\ell}]

\displaystyle\leq\exp{\bigg(}\frac{(1+o(1))t^{2}}{2n(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v^{*}+h))}{\bigg)},

which alternatively implies that

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{U}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{U}_{n}]\|_{\psi_{2}}=n^{1/2}\|\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}]\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq(1+o(1))(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v^{*}+h))^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(SA-3)

The Critical Temperature Regime.

Then we study the critical temperature regime with
\beta=1
. Note that one has
\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{U}_{n}]=0
and for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\textbf{}F(v)

\displaystyle=F(0)+F^{\prime}(0)v+\frac{1}{2}F^{(2)}(0)v^{2}+\frac{1}{6}F^{(3)}(0)v^{3}+\frac{1}{24}F^{(4)}(0)v^{4}+O(v^{5})

\displaystyle=F(0)+\frac{1}{12}v^{4}+O(v^{5}).

Then we can obtain that
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[V^{2\ell}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}v^{2\ell}\exp(-nF(v))dv}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp(-nF(v))dv}=(1+o(1))\cdot 2^{\ell-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot 3^{\frac{\ell}{2}+\frac{1}{4}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\ell}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right)}{\Gamma(1/4)}

\displaystyle\leq(1+o(1))\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}{\bigg(}\frac{2^{3/2}\cdot 3^{3/4}\Gamma(3/4)}{n^{1/2}\Gamma(1/4)}{\bigg)}^{\ell}\Gamma\left(\frac{2\ell+1}{2}\right).

And we immediately obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(t\mathsf{V}_{n})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{\ell}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}^{2\ell}]}{\Gamma(1+\ell)}\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{1+o(1)}{\Gamma(1+2\ell)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}{\bigg(}\frac{2^{1/2}\cdot 3^{3/4}\sqrt{2}\Gamma(3/4)}{n^{1/2}\Gamma(1/4)}{\bigg)}^{\ell}\Gamma\left(\frac{2\ell+1}{2}\right)t^{\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\exp{\bigg(}\frac{1+o(1)}{2}t^{2}{\bigg(}\frac{2^{3/2}\cdot 3^{3/4}\Gamma(3/4)}{n^{1/2}\Gamma(1/4)}{\bigg)}{\bigg)},

which finally leads to

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{V}_{n}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq(1+o(1))\sqrt{\frac{2^{1/2}\cdot 3^{3/4}\Gamma(3/4)}{n^{1/2}\Gamma(1/4)}}.

(SA-4)

The Low Temperature Regime.

We shall note that at the low temperature regime the function
F(v)
has two symmetric global minima
v_{1}>0>v_{2}
, satisfying

\displaystyle F^{\prime}(v_{1})=F^{\prime}(v_{2})=0\quad\Rightarrow\quad v_{\ell}=\sqrt{\beta}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ell}+h)\quad\text{ for }\ell\in\{1,2\}.

Then we can check that by the Laplace method, for all
t>0
(following the path given by the high temperature regime) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(t(\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]))|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{[0,\infty)}\exp\left(t(v-v_{1})-nF(v)\right)dv}{\int_{[0,\infty)}\exp(-nF(v))dv}

\displaystyle=\exp{\bigg(}\frac{(1+o(1))t^{2}}{2n(1-\sqrt{\beta}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{1}))}{\bigg)}.

Then we similarly obtain that

\mathbb{E}[\exp(t(\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}|\mathsf{V}_{n}<0]))|\mathsf{V}_{n}<0]=\exp\left(\frac{(1+o(1))t^{2}}{2n(1-\sqrt{\beta}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{1}))}\right)
. Hence we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}|\mathsf{V}_{n}<0]|\mathsf{V}_{n}<0\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\|\mathsf{V}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+o(1))(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{1}))^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(SA-5)

The Drifting Sequence Case.

Then we consider the drifting case.

First consider
\beta=1-cn^{-\frac{1}{2}}
with
c\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
\beta\geq 0
. We will show that for any fixed
n
,
\lVert W_{n}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}
is increasing in
\beta
when
\beta\in[0,1]
. This will imply that in the drifting case,
\lVert W_{n}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}
will be no larger than its value at the critical regime.

For a comparison argument, denote
F_{\beta}(v)=-\frac{1}{2}v^{2}+\log\cosh(\sqrt{\beta}v)
. Let
0<\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}\leq 1
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\exp(nF_{\beta_{2}}(v))}{\exp(nF_{\beta_{1}}(v))}=\exp(n\log\cosh(\sqrt{\beta_{2}}v)-n\log\cosh(\sqrt{\beta_{1}}v)),

where

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dv}\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{\beta_{2}}v)}{\cosh(\sqrt{\beta_{1}}v)}=\frac{(\sqrt{\beta_{2}}-\sqrt{\beta_{1}})\sinh((\sqrt{\beta_{2}}-\sqrt{\beta_{1}})v)}{\cosh^{2}(\sqrt{\beta_{1}}v)}>0.

Hence for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\beta}(|W_{n}|\geq t)=2\frac{\int_{t}^{\infty}\exp(nF_{\beta}(v))dv}{\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(nF_{\beta}(v))dv}

increases as
\beta\in[0,1]
increases. This shows that
\lVert W_{n}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}
increases as
\beta\in[0,1]
increases. Together with Equation (
SA-4
), we have under
\beta_{n}=1-\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}
,
0\leq c\leq\sqrt{n}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathsf{V}_{n}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\leq(1+o(1))\sqrt{\frac{2^{1/2}\cdot 3^{3/4}\Gamma(3/4)}{n^{1/2}\Gamma(1/4)}},

where
o(\cdot)
is by an absolute constant.

Then we consider
\beta=1+cn^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. We shall note that under this situation it is not hard to check that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\mathsf{V}_{n})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\mathsf{V}_{n})|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]+\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\mathsf{V}_{n})|\mathsf{V}_{n}<0]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbb{E}[\exp(t(\mathsf{V}_{n}-v_{+}))|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]\exp(tv_{+})+\mathbb{E}[\exp(t(\mathsf{V}_{n}-v_{-}))|\mathsf{V}_{n}<0]\exp(tv_{-})\right).

Then, under this case we have by Taylor expanding
F
at
0
and the fact that
\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}}|F^{(5)}(v)|<\infty
,

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{V}_{n}}(v)\propto\sum_{l\in\{-,+\}}\mathbbm{1}(v\in\mathscr{C}_{l})\exp{\bigg(}-cn^{\frac{1}{2}}(v-v_{l})^{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3c}}{3}n^{\frac{3}{4}}(v-v_{l})^{3}-\frac{1}{12}n(v-v_{l})^{4}-O(n(v-v_{l})^{5}){\bigg)}.

Before we start to upper bound the moments, we first use the fact that
v_{+}=O(n^{-1/4})
to obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{(-v_{+},0)}v^{2\ell}\exp\left(-\sqrt{3c}v^{3}\right)dv\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}v_{+}^{2\ell}\exp(-\sqrt{3c}n^{-1/4})=O\left(n^{-1/4-\ell/2}\right).

Then we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{V}_{n}-v_{+})^{2\ell}|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]=n^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\frac{\int_{(-v_{+},+\infty)}v^{2\ell}\exp\left(-cv^{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3c}}{3}v^{3}-\frac{1}{12}v^{4}\right)dv}{\int_{(-v_{+},+\infty)}\exp\left(-cv^{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3c}}{3}v^{3}-\frac{1}{12}v^{4}\right)dv}(1+o(1))

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}(1+o(1))\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}v^{2\ell}\exp(-3cv^{2})dv+\int_{(-v_{+},+\infty)}v^{2\ell}\exp(-\sqrt{3c}v^{3})dv+\int_{\mathbb{R}}v^{2\ell}\exp(-\frac{1}{4}v^{4})dv}{\int_{(-v_{+},+\infty)}\exp\left(-cv^{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3c}}{3}v^{3}-\frac{1}{12}v^{4}\right)dv}

\displaystyle=n^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}(1+o(1))\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}v^{2\ell}\exp(-3cv^{2})dv+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}v^{2\ell}\exp(-\sqrt{3c}v^{3})dv+\int_{\mathbb{R}}v^{2\ell}\exp(-\frac{1}{4}v^{4})dv}{\int_{(-v_{+},+\infty)}\exp\left(-cv^{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3c}}{3}v^{3}-\frac{1}{12}v^{4}\right)dv}+O(n^{-1/4-\ell/2})

\displaystyle=n^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}(1+o(1)){\bigg(}\mathscr{C}_{3}{\bigg(}\frac{1}{3c}{\bigg)}^{\ell}\Gamma{\bigg(}\ell+\frac{1}{2}{\bigg)}+\mathscr{C}_{4}(3c)^{-\frac{\ell}{3}}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{2\ell}{3}+\frac{1}{3}{\bigg)}+\mathscr{C}_{5}2^{\ell}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{\ell}{2}+\frac{1}{4}{\bigg)}{\bigg)},

with

\mathscr{C}_{3}:=\frac{(3c)^{-1/2}}{3\int_{(-v_{+},+\infty)}\exp\left(-cv^{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3c}}{3}v^{3}-\frac{1}{12}v^{4}\right)dv}
,
\mathscr{C}_{4}=\frac{1}{9\int_{(-v_{+},+\infty)}\exp\left(-cv^{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3c}}{3}v^{3}-\frac{1}{12}v^{4}\right)dv}
,

and
\mathscr{C}_{5}=\frac{2^{-3/2}}{\int_{(-v_{+},+\infty)}\exp\left(-cv^{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3c}}{3}v^{3}-\frac{1}{12}v^{4}\right)dv}
.
Therefore, we can simply use the definition of the m.g.f. to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(t^{2}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{V}_{n}-v_{+})^{2})|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{{2\ell}}\mathbb{E}[(\mathsf{V}_{n}-v_{+})^{2\ell}|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]}{\Gamma(2\ell+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{(1+o(1))n^{-\ell/2}t^{2\ell}}{\Gamma(2\ell+1)}{\bigg(}\mathscr{C}_{3}{\bigg(}\frac{1}{3c}{\bigg)}^{\ell}\Gamma{\bigg(}\ell+\frac{1}{2}{\bigg)}+\mathscr{C}_{4}(3c)^{-\frac{\ell}{3}}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{2\ell}{3}+\frac{1}{3}{\bigg)}+\mathscr{C}_{5}2^{\ell}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{\ell}{2}+\frac{1}{4}{\bigg)}{\bigg)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{(1+o(1))n^{-\ell/2}t^{2\ell}}{\Gamma(2\ell+1)}{\bigg(}\mathscr{C}_{3}({3c})^{-1}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{3}{2}{\bigg)}+\mathscr{C}_{4}(3c)^{-1/3}\Gamma(1)+2\mathscr{C}_{5}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{3}{4}{\bigg)}{\bigg)}^{\ell}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{2\ell+1}{2}{\bigg)}

\displaystyle\leq(1-2t^{2}n^{1/2}/\sigma^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\sigma:={\bigg(}\mathscr{C}_{3}({3c})^{-1}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{3}{2}{\bigg)}+\mathscr{C}_{4}(3c)^{-1/3}\Gamma(1)+2\mathscr{C}_{5}\Gamma{\bigg(}\frac{3}{4}{\bigg)}{\bigg)}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then we use the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{n}|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]=v_{+}
to obtain that (here we use proposition 2.5.2 in
(
12
)
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(t(\mathsf{V}_{n}-v_{+}))|\mathsf{V}_{n}>0]\leq\exp\left(18e^{2}n^{-1/2}\sigma^{2}t^{2}\right).

Similarly one obtains that

\mathbb{E}[\exp(t(\mathsf{V}_{n}-v_{-}))|\mathsf{V}_{n}<0]\leq\exp(18e^{2}n^{-1/2}\sigma^{2}t^{2})
.
And hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\mathsf{V}_{n})]\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\exp(tv_{+})+\exp(-tv_{+})\right)\exp(18e^{2}n^{-1/2}\sigma^{2}t^{2})\leq\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}t^{2}v_{+}^{2}\right).

SA-8.3
Proof for Lemma
SA-3
High Temperature

We will leverage the representation of
\mathbf{W}
as a mixture of independent Bernouli random variables after conditioning on some latent variable
\mathsf{U}_{n}
. We take
\mathsf{U}_{n}
to be a random variable with density

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}}(u)=\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+n\log\cosh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}u+h\right)\right)}{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}v^{2}+n\log\cosh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}v+h\right)\right)dv}

(SA-6)

Using Gaussian integral identity
\exp(v^{2}/2)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp\left(-u^{2}/2+uv\right)du
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{w}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\exp\left(\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}u+h\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\right)\right)}{2^{n}\exp\left(n\log\cosh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}u+h\right)\right)}f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}}(u)du.

(SA-7)

Hence condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
\mathbf{W}_{i}
are i.i.d Bernouli with
\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=1|\mathsf{U}_{n})=\frac{1}{2}(\tanh(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h)+1)
, and

\displaystyle e(\mathsf{U}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)|\mathsf{U}_{n}\right]

(SA-8)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}\right]\bigg(\tanh\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h\bigg)-\pi\bigg),

(SA-9)

\displaystyle v(\mathsf{U}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{V}\left[X_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)|\mathsf{U}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\right]\left\{\frac{(1-\pi)^{2}}{2}(\tanh(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h)+1)+\frac{(1+\pi)^{2}}{2}(1-\tanh(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h))\right\}

\displaystyle\phantom{v(\mathsf{U}_{n})=}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}\right]^{2}\left(\tanh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h\right)-\pi\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{V}[X_{i}]\min\left\{(1-\pi)^{2},(1+\pi)^{2}\right\}=:C_{2}\mathbb{V}[X_{i}],

Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{i}^{3}(W_{i}-\pi)^{3}\right||\mathsf{U}_{n}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{i}|^{3}\right]\max\left\{(1-\pi)^{3},(1+\pi)^{3}\right\}=:C_{3}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{i}|^{3}\right].

Step 1: Conditional Berry-Esseen

Apply Berry-Esseen Theorem conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(G_{n}\leq t|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{t-\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u\right]}{\mathbb{V}\left[X_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u\right]^{1/2}}\right)\right|\leq 3\frac{C_{3}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{i}|^{3}\right]}{C_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathbb{V}\left[X_{i}\right]^{\frac{3}{2}}}n^{-1/2}.

Take
Z\sim N(0,1)
independent to
\mathbf{W}
and
X_{i}
’s.
\mathsf{U}_{n}
is sub-Gaussian by Equation
SA-3
, hence

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(G_{n},v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\mathbb{P}\left(G_{n}\leq t\middle|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{t-\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})}{v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}}\right))f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}}(u)du\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3\frac{C_{3}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{i}|^{3}\right]}{C_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\right]^{\frac{3}{2}}}n^{-1/2}.

Step 2: Stabilization of Variance

By independence between
\mathsf{U}_{n}
and
Z
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})),\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi\left(\frac{t-\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})}{v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}}\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{t-\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})}{\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\phi\left(\frac{t-\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})}{v^{\ast}(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}}\right)(t-\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n}))\left(v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{-1/2}-\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{-1/2}\right)\right|\right],

where
v^{\ast}(\mathsf{U}_{n})
is some quantity between
\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]
and
v(\mathsf{U}_{n})
, and by Equation
SA-8
,
v^{\ast}(\mathsf{U}_{n})\geq C_{2}\mathbb{V}[X_{i}]
. It follows from boundedness of
v(\mathsf{U}_{n})
and Lipshitzness of
\tanh
in the expression of
v(\mathsf{U}_{n})
that

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})),\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\phi\left(\frac{t-\sqrt{n}e(u)}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]2(\pi^{2}+1)}}\right)(t-\sqrt{n}e(u))\right|\frac{1}{2\sqrt{C_{2}\mathbb{V}[X_{i}]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|v(\mathsf{U}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]\right|\right]=O(n^{-1/2}).

Step 3: Reduction Through TV-distance Inequality

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n}),\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{TV}}\left(\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n}),\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{TV}}\left(e(\mathsf{U}_{n}),e(\mathsf{U}+b_{n})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\leq}}d_{\operatorname{TV}}\left(\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathsf{U}+b_{n}\right),

where
b_{n}=\sqrt{n}v_{0}
. The first inequality is by relation between KS- and TV-distances. For the second inequality, denote
X=\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})
,
Y=\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})
. Denote by
f_{X},f_{Y},f_{Z}
the Lebesgue density of
X,Y,Z
respectively. Then using
Z\raisebox{0.50003pt}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{$\models$}}X
and
Z\raisebox{0.50003pt}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{$\models$}}Y
, by data processing inequality,

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{TV}}\left(Z+X,Z+Y\right)\leq d_{\operatorname{TV}}(X,Y).

Above proves inequality (2). Inequality (3) is by scale-invariance of TV distance and data processing inequality.

Step 4: Gaussian Approximation for
\mathsf{U}_{n}

Consider
\mathsf{V}_{n}=n^{-1/2}\mathsf{U}_{n}
. Then

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{V}_{n}}(v)\propto\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}nv^{2}+n\log\cosh\left(\sqrt{\beta}v+h\right)\right)=:\exp\left(-n\phi(v)\right),

where
\phi(v)=-\frac{1}{2}v^{2}+\log\cosh(\sqrt{\beta}v+h)
.
\phi
is maximized at
v_{0}
that solves

\displaystyle v_{0}=\sqrt{\beta}\tanh\left(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h\right).

(SA-10)

We will approximate the integral of
f_{\mathsf{V}_{n}}
by Laplace method. We will introduce constants
c_{0},c_{1}
and
c_{2}
that only depends on
\beta
and
h
. By Equation (5.1.21) in
[
2
]
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp\left(-n\phi(v)\right)dv

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{n\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}}\exp\left(-n\phi(v_{0})\right)+O\left(\frac{\exp(-n\phi(v_{0}))}{n^{3/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{n\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}}\exp\left(-n\phi(v_{0})\right)\left[1+O(n^{-1})\right],

where the
O(n^{-1})
term only depends on
n
and
\phi
. It follows that

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{V}_{n}}(v)=\sqrt{\frac{n\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-n\phi(v)+n\phi(v_{0})\right)\left[1+O(n^{-1})\right].

Then by a change of variable and the fact that
O(n^{-1})
term does not depend on
v
,

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}}(u)=\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-n\phi(n^{-1/2}u)+n\phi(n^{-1/2}u_{0})\right)[1+O(n^{-1})].

(SA-11)

Taylor expanding
\phi
at
v_{0}=n^{-1/2}u_{0}
and using
\phi^{\prime}(v_{0})=0
, we get

\displaystyle-n\phi(n^{-1/2}u)+n\phi(n^{-1/2}u_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2}(u-u_{0})^{2}-\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ast}+h)\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ast}+h)\frac{(u-u_{0})^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}}

(SA-12)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2}\right)(u-u_{0})^{2}-\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ast}+h)\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ast}+h)\frac{(u-u_{0})^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}},

(SA-13)

where
v_{\ast}
is some quantity between
v_{0}
and
n^{-1/2}u
. Now take
b_{n}=u_{0}=\sqrt{n}v_{0}
and take
\mathsf{U}\sim N(0,(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})^{-1})
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{TV}}(\mathsf{U}_{n},b_{n}+\mathsf{U})=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}}(u)-f_{b_{n}+U}(u)\right|du

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})(u-u_{0})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\exp\left(-\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ast}(u)+h)\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ast}(u)+h)\frac{(u-u_{0})^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}}\right)-1\right]du\left[1+O(n^{-1})\right],

where
v^{\ast}(u)
is some random quantity between
v_{0}=n^{-1/2}u_{0}
and
n^{-1/2}u
. We will show that we can restrict the analysis to the region
[u_{0}-c_{0}\sqrt{\log n},u_{0}+c_{0}\sqrt{\log n}]
, which is where the bulk of mass lies. Since
\mathsf{U}\sim N(u_{0},(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})^{-1})
, for some constant
c
only depending on
\beta
and
h
,
\mathbb{P}\left(\left|b_{n}+\mathsf{U}-u_{0}\right|\geq c\sqrt{\log n}\right)\leq n^{-1}
. Using a change of variable and concavity of
\phi
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mathsf{U}_{n}-u_{0}\right|\geq c\sqrt{\log n}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\mathsf{V}_{n}-v_{0}\right|\geq c\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[v_{0}-\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}},v_{0}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}]}\sqrt{\frac{n\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-n(\phi(v)-\phi(v_{0}))\right)\left[1+O(n^{-1})\right]dv

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[v_{0}-\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}},v_{0}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}]}\sqrt{\frac{n\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-nc_{1}(v-v_{0})^{2})\right)\left[1+O(n^{-1})\right]dv

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[-\sqrt{\log n},\sqrt{\log n}]}\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-c_{1}s^{2})\right)\left[1+O(n^{-1})\right]ds=O(n^{-1}).

In the third line we used the fact that
\phi(v_{0}+t)-\phi(v_{0})=\int_{0}^{t}\phi^{\prime}(v_{0}+s)ds
and the first derivative is bounded by

\displaystyle|\phi^{\prime}(v_{0}+s)|

\displaystyle=|v_{0}+s-\sqrt{\beta}\tanh\left(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+\sqrt{\beta}s+h\right)|

(SA-14)

\displaystyle=|s+\sqrt{\beta}\tanh\left(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h\right)-\sqrt{\beta}\tanh\left(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+\sqrt{\beta}s+h\right)|

(SA-15)

\displaystyle\geq|s|\left(1-\sech^{2}(w_{0})\right),

(SA-16)

where
w_{0}
is the solution to
\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h)-\tanh(w_{0})=(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h-w_{0})\sech^{2}(w_{0})
. It follows that
\phi(v)-\phi(v_{0})\leq-\frac{1}{2}(1-\sech(w_{0})^{2})(v-v_{0})^{2}
. Using boundedness of
\tanh
and
\sech
and the Lipschitzness of
\exp
when restricted to
[-1,1]
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{TV}}(\mathsf{U}_{n},b_{n}+\mathsf{U})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{u_{0}-c\sqrt{\log n}}^{u_{0}+c\sqrt{\log n}}\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})(u-u_{0})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\exp\left(-\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ast}(u)+h)\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{\ast}(u)+h)\frac{(u-u_{0})^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}}\right)-1\right]du\left[1+O(n^{-1})\right]+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{u_{0}-c\sqrt{\log n}}^{u_{0}+c\sqrt{\log n}}\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}(v_{0})}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})(u-u_{0})^{2}\right)c_{2}\frac{|u-u_{0}|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}du\left[1+O(n^{-1})\right]+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(n^{-1/2}).

Step 5: Gaussian Approximation for
\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})

In this step, we will show that
\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})
can be well-approximated by
\sqrt{\beta}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h)\mathsf{U}
and hence
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)
can be well-approximated by a Gaussian.

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U}),\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{\beta}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h)\mathsf{U}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi\left(\frac{t-\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})}{\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}}\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{t-\sqrt{\beta}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h)U}{\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}}{\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})-\sqrt{\beta}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h)\mathsf{U}\right|\right]

Since
d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathsf{U})=O(n^{-1/2})
and
\pi=\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h\right)\right]
, Taylor expanding
\tanh
at
\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\sqrt{n}\left[\tanh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}(\sqrt{n}v_{0}+\mathsf{U})+h\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}(\sqrt{n}v_{0}+\mathsf{U})+h\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\sqrt{\beta}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}v_{0}+h)\mathsf{U}+O\left(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{U}^{2}\right)+O(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\sqrt{\beta}(1-\frac{v_{0}^{2}}{\beta})\mathsf{U}+O\left(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{U}^{2}\right)+O(n^{-1/2}),

It follows that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U})-\sqrt{\beta}(1-\frac{v_{0}^{2}}{\beta})\mathsf{U}\right|\right]=O(n^{-1/2})
and hence

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\sqrt{n}e(b_{n}+\mathsf{U}),\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\sqrt{\beta}(1-\frac{v_{0}^{2}}{\beta})\mathsf{U}\right)=O(n^{-1/2}).

Recall
\mathsf{U}\sim N(0,(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})^{-1})
, hence
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\sqrt{\beta}(1-\frac{v_{0}^{2}}{\beta})\mathsf{U}\sim N(0,\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]^{2}\frac{(\beta-v_{0})^{2}}{\beta(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})})
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]\mathbb{E}[(W_{i}-\pi)^{2}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]]-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]^{2}\mathbb{E}[W_{i}-\pi|\mathsf{U}_{n}]^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}](1-\pi^{2})-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]^{2}(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]^{2}-\pi^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}](1-\pi^{2})+O(n^{-1/2}),

where the last line is because
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]=\tanh(\sqrt{\beta/n}\mathsf{U}_{n})
and
\mathsf{U}_{n}
is sub-Gaussian.
Since
Z\raisebox{0.50003pt}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{$\models$}}\mathsf{U}
,

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}[v(\mathsf{U}_{n})]^{1/2}Z+\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\sqrt{\beta}(1-\frac{v_{0}^{2}}{\beta})U,N(0,\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}](1-\pi^{2})+\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]^{2}\frac{(\beta-v_{0}^{2})^{2}}{\beta(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})})\bigg)=O(n^{-1/2}).

Combining the previous five steps, we get

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}(W_{i}-\pi),N\left(0,\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}](1-\pi^{2})+\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]^{2}\frac{(\beta-v_{0}^{2})^{2}}{\beta(1-\beta+v_{0}^{2})}\right)\bigg)=O(n^{-1/2}).

SA-8.4
Proof for Lemma
SA-3
Critical Temperature

Throughout the proof, we denote by
\mathtt{C}
an absolute constant, and
\mathtt{K}
a constant that only depends on the distribution of
X_{i}
. The proofs for the critical temperature case will have a similar structure as the proof for the high temperature case, based the same
\mathsf{U}_{n}
defined in Equation (
SA-6
).

Step 1: Conditional Berry-Esseen.

The same argument as in the high-temperature case gives

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(\mathscr{g}_{n},v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})\right)\leq\mathtt{K}n^{-1/2}.

Step 2: Approximation for
\mathsf{U}_{n}
.

Take
\mathsf{W}
to be a random variable with density function

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{W}}(z)=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3^{1/4}\Gamma(\frac{1}{4})}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{12}z^{4}\right),\quad z\in\mathbb{R},

independent to
\mathsf{Z}
. Take
\mathsf{W}_{n}=n^{-1/4}\mathsf{U}_{n}
and
\mathsf{V}_{n}=n^{-1/2}\mathsf{U}_{n}
. Again
f_{\mathsf{V}_{n}}(v)\propto\exp(-n\phi(v))
,
where
\phi(v):=-\frac{1}{2}v^{2}+\log\cosh(v)
. In particular,
\phi^{(v)}(0)=0
for all
0\leq v\leq 3
, and
\phi^{(4)}(0)=-2<0
,
\phi^{(5)}(0)=0
,
\phi^{(6)}(0)=16>0
. Example 5.2.1 in
[
2
]
leads to

f_{\mathsf{V}_{n}}(v)=n^{\frac{1}{4}}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3^{\frac{1}{4}}\Gamma(\frac{1}{4})}\exp(n\phi(v)-n\phi(0))(1+o(1)),

which implies
f_{\mathsf{W}_{n}}(w)=f_{W}(w)(1+o(1))
. Results in
[
2
]
do not give a rate, however. We will use a more cumbersome approach to obtain a slightly sub-optimal rate.

By a change of variable,
f_{\mathsf{W}_{n}}(w)=\frac{h_{n}(w)}{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h_{n}(u)du}
, where
h_{n}
can be written as

\displaystyle h_{n}(w)=\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}w^{2}+n\log\cosh\left(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}w\right)\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{12}w^{4}+g(w)n^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{6}\right).

The last equality follows from Taylor expanding the term in
\exp(\cdot)
at
w=0
, and
g
is some bounded function.

\displaystyle\int_{-10\sqrt{\log n}}^{10\sqrt{\log n}}h_{n}(w)dw

\displaystyle=I_{n}(1+O((\log n)^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}})),\quad I_{n}:=\int_{-10\sqrt{\log n}}^{10\sqrt{\log n}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{12}w^{4}\bigg)dw

Moreover,
\int_{[-10\sqrt{\log n},10\sqrt{\log n}]^{c}}h_{n}(w)dw=O(n^{-1/2})=I_{n}[1+O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})]
. Hence for denominator, we have
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h_{n}(w)dw=I_{n}[1+O((\log n)^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}})]
. It follows that

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{TV}}(\mathsf{W}_{n},\mathsf{W})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{-10\sqrt{\log n}}^{10\sqrt{\log n}}I_{n}^{-1}\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{12}w^{4}\bigg)n^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{6}dw+\int_{-10\sqrt{\log n}}^{10\sqrt{\log n}}I_{n}^{-1}O((\log n)^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}})dw

\displaystyle+P(|\mathsf{W}_{n}|\geq 10\sqrt{\log n})+\mathbb{P}(|\mathsf{W}|\geq 10\sqrt{\log n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O((\log n)^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Step 3: Data Processing Inequality.

We can use data processing inequality to get

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n}),v(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W})^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}+\sqrt{n}e(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W})\right)\leq d_{\operatorname{TV}}\left(\mathsf{W}_{n},\mathsf{W}\right)=O(n^{-1/2}).

Step 4: Non-Gaussian Approximation for
n^{\frac{1}{4}}e(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W})

\displaystyle n^{1/4}e(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W}))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]n^{\frac{1}{4}}\tanh\left(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W}\right)=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\left[\mathsf{W}-O\left(\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{3\sqrt{n}}\right)\right],

where we have use the fact that
\tanh^{(2)}(0)=0
.
Hence there exists
C>0
such that for
n
large enough, for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\left[\mathsf{W}+C\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]\leq t\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(n^{1/4}e(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W}))\leq t\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\left[\mathsf{W}-C\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]\leq t\right).

(0)

We have showed that there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\mathsf{W}|\geq c\sqrt{\log n})\leq n^{-1/2},

(1)

in which case
\mathsf{W}^{2}/\sqrt{n}\leq 1
for large enough
n
. Hence for large enough
n
if
t/\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]>c\sqrt{\log n}+1
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}+C\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]},|\mathsf{W}|\leq c\sqrt{\log n}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]},|\mathsf{W}|\leq c\sqrt{\log n}\right)=0.

(2)

If
0<t/\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]<c\sqrt{\log n}+1
, then

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}+\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]},|\mathsf{W}|\leq c\sqrt{\log n}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]},|\mathsf{W}|\leq c\sqrt{\log n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]}\leq\mathsf{W}\leq\frac{1-\sqrt{1-4n^{-1/2}t/\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]}}{2n^{-1/2}},|\mathsf{W}|\leq c\sqrt{\log n}\right).

Now we study
g(x;\alpha)=(1-\sqrt{1-4x\alpha})/(2x),x>0
. Then
\sup_{\alpha\leq\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{0\leq x\leq\frac{1}{2}}|\theta^{\prime}(x;\alpha)|\leq 2
and
g(0;\alpha)=\alpha
. Since for large enough
n
,
0<t/\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]<c\sqrt{\log n}+1\leq\frac{1}{4}
and
0\leq n^{-1/2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we have
\frac{1-\sqrt{1-4n^{-1/2}t/\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]}}{2n^{-1/2}}\leq t/\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]+2n^{-1/2}
. Hence if
0<t/\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]<c\sqrt{\log n}+1
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}+\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]},|\mathsf{W}|\leq c\sqrt{\log n}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]},|\mathsf{W}|\leq c\sqrt{\log n}\right)\right|=O(n^{-1/2}).

(3)

Combining (1), (2), (3),

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}+\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]}\right)\right|=O(n^{-1/2}).

By similar argument, we can show

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}-\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\mathsf{W}\leq\frac{t}{\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]}\right)\right|=O(n^{-1/2}).

Noticing that
W
and
-W
have the same distribution, the above two inequalities also hold for
t\leq 0
. Hence it follows from (0) that

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(n^{1/4}e(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W})),\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\mathsf{W}\right)=O(n^{-1/2}).

Step 5: Vanishing Variance Term.

Denote by
f_{\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{Z}}
the density of
\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{Z}
. Then

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{Z}}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3^{1/4}\Gamma(\frac{1}{4})}\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{12}(y-x)^{4}\bigg)\frac{\exp(-\sqrt{n}x^{2}/2)}{\sqrt{2\pi n^{-1/2}}}dx.

We will use Laplace method to show
f_{\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{\mathsf{Z}}}
is close to
f_{\mathsf{W}}
. However, to get uniformity over
y
, we need to work harder than in the high temperature case. Define
\varphi(x)=x^{2}/2
and
g_{y}(t)=\exp(-(t-y)^{4}/12)
. Consider

\displaystyle I_{y,+}(\lambda)=\int_{0}^{\infty}g_{y}(t)\exp(-\lambda\varphi(t))dt,\qquad I_{y,-}(\lambda)=\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{y}(t)\exp(-\lambda\varphi(t))dt.

Following Section 5.1 in
[
2
]
, take
\tau>0
such that
\varphi(t)=\tau
, by a change of variable,

\displaystyle I_{y,+}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\exp(-\lambda\varphi(0))\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg[\frac{g_{y}(t)}{\varphi^{\prime}(t)}\bigg|_{t=\varphi^{-1}(\tau)}\bigg]\exp(-\lambda\tau)d\tau=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\exp(-(\sqrt{2\tau}-y)^{4}/12)}{\sqrt{2\tau}}\exp(-\lambda\tau)d\tau.

To get rate of convergence uniformly in
y
, we follow the proof of Watson’s Lemma but consider only up to first order term. Taylor expanding
x\mapsto\exp(-x^{4})/12
up to first order at
y
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\exp(-(\sqrt{2\tau}-y)^{4}/12)}{\sqrt{2\tau}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp(-y^{4}/12)}{\sqrt{2\tau}}+\frac{1}{3}\exp(-y^{4}/12)y^{3}+\frac{h_{y}(\tau^{\ast})}{2}\sqrt{2\tau},

where
\tau^{\ast}
is some quantity between
0
and
\sqrt{2\tau}
and

\displaystyle h_{y}(u)=-\exp(-(u-y)^{4}/12)(u-y)^{2}+\frac{1}{9}\exp(-(u-y)^{4}/12)(u-y)^{6}.

In particular, we have
\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}|h_{y}(u)|<C
for some absolute constant
C
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{h_{y}(\tau^{\ast})}{2}\sqrt{2\tau}\exp(-\lambda\tau)d\tau\bigg|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{2}}\Gamma\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)\lambda^{-3/2},\quad\forall\lambda>0.

Evaluating the first two terms, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|I_{y,+}(\lambda)-\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2\lambda}}\exp(-y^{4}/12)-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{3}\exp(-y^{4}/12)y^{3}\exp(-\lambda\tau)d\tau\bigg|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{2}}\Gamma\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)\lambda^{-3/2},\forall\lambda>0.

Similarly, for
I_{y,-}
, change of variable by taking
\tau<0
such that
\varphi(t)=\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|I_{y,-}(\lambda)-\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2\lambda}}\exp(-y^{4}/12)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{3}\exp(-y^{4}/12)y^{3}\exp(-\lambda\tau)d\tau\bigg|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{2}}\Gamma\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)\lambda^{-3/2},\forall\lambda>0.

Combining the two parts, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}g_{y}(t)\exp(-\lambda\varphi(t))dt-\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{\lambda}}\exp(-y^{4}/12)\bigg|\leq C\sqrt{2}\Gamma\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\bigg)\lambda^{-3/2},\quad\forall\lambda>0.

Now take
\lambda=\sqrt{n}
and multiply both sides by
\frac{n^{1/4}}{3^{1/4}\Gamma(\frac{1}{4})\sqrt{\pi}}
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|f_{\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{Z}}(y)-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3^{1/4}\Gamma(\frac{1}{4})}\exp(-y^{4}/12)\bigg|\leq C\frac{\sqrt{2}\Gamma(\frac{3}{2})}{3^{1/4}\Gamma(\frac{1}{4})\sqrt{\pi}}n^{-1/2}.

By a truncation argument, we have

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{Z},\mathsf{W})

\displaystyle\leq d_{\operatorname{TV}}(\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{Z},\mathsf{W})

\displaystyle=\int_{-\sqrt{\log n}}^{\sqrt{\log n}}|f_{\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{Z}}(y)-f_{\mathsf{W}}(y)|dy+\mathbb{P}(|\mathsf{W}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{Z}|\geq\sqrt{\log n})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{P}(|\mathsf{W}|\geq\sqrt{\log n})

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{n^{-1}\log n}.

Together with the fact that

\displaystyle n^{-1/4}v(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W})

\displaystyle=n^{-1/4}(\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]^{2}\tanh^{2}(\sqrt{\beta}n^{-1/4}\mathsf{W}))^{1/2}

\displaystyle=n^{-1/4}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]^{1/2}(1+O_{\psi_{2}}(n^{-1/4})),

we know

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}(n^{-1/4}v(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W})^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}+n^{1/4}e(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W}),\mathsf{W})=O(\sqrt{\log n}n^{-1/2}).

Putting together all previous steps, we have

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}(n^{1/4}\mathscr{g}_{n},\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\mathsf{W})=O((\log n)^{3}n^{-1/2}).

SA-8.5
Proof for Lemma
SA-3
Low Temperature

Throughout the proof, we denote by
\mathtt{C}
an absolute constant, and
\mathtt{K}
a constant that only depends on the distribution of
X_{i}
. The proofs are based on essentially the same argument as in the high temperature case.

Instead of using sub-Gaussianity of
\mathsf{U}_{n}
, here we use
\mathsf{U}_{n}
is sub-Gaussian condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{\ell}
,
\ell\in\{-,+\}
. In particular, the previous step 2 by:

Step 2: Approximation for
\mathsf{U}_{n}
.

In case
\beta>1
,
\phi(v)=\frac{1}{2}v^{2}-\log(\cosh(\sqrt{\beta}v))
has two global minimum
v_{+}
and
v_{-}
, which are the two solutions of
v-\sqrt{\beta}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}v)=0
. We want to show
\phi^{(2)}(v_{+})=\phi^{(2)}(v_{-})=1-\beta+v_{+}^{2}>0
. It sufffices to show
v_{+}>\sqrt{\beta-1}
. Since
\phi^{\prime}(v)<0
for
v\in(0,v_{+})
and
\phi^{\prime}(v)>0
for
v\in(v_{+},\infty)
, it suffices to show
\phi^{\prime}(\sqrt{\beta-1})<0
. But

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}(\sqrt{\beta-1})<0\Leftrightarrow\sqrt{\beta-1}-\sqrt{\beta}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta(\beta-1)})<0\Leftrightarrow\beta>1.

Hence
\phi^{(2)}(v_{+})=\phi^{(2)}(v_{-})>0
. Observe that on
\pazocal{I}_{-}=(-\infty,0)
and
\pazocal{I}_{+}=(0,\infty)
respectively, the absolute minimum of
\phi
occurs at
v_{-}
and
v_{+}
, and
\phi^{\prime}
is non-zero on
\pazocal{I}_{-}
and
\pazocal{I}_{+}
except at
v_{-}
and
v_{+}
. Hence we can apply Laplace method (Equation 5.1.21 in
[
2
]
) sperarately on
\pazocal{I}_{-}
and
\pazocal{I}_{+}
to get

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\exp(-n\phi(v))dv=\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{n\phi^{(2)}(v_{-})}}\exp(-n\phi(v_{-}))(1+O(n^{-1})),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-n\phi(v))dv=\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{n\phi^{(2)}(v_{+})}}\exp(-n\phi(v_{+}))(1+O(n^{-1})).

It follows from the definition of
f_{\mathsf{V}_{n}}
and a change of variable that the density of
\mathsf{U}_{n}=\sqrt{n}\mathsf{V}_{n}
can be approximated by

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}}(u)=\sum_{l=+,-}\mathbbm{1}(u\in\mathscr{C}_{l})\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{(2)}(v_{-})}{8\pi}}\exp(-n\phi(n^{-1/2}u)+n\phi(n^{-1/2}u_{l}))(1+O(n^{-1})),

where
u_{l}=\sqrt{n}v_{l},l\in\{+,-\}
. Since
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{U}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{+})=\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{U}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{-})=\frac{1}{2}
, condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{+}
,

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}|\mathsf{U}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{+}}(u)=\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{(2)}(v_{+})}{2\pi}}\exp(-n\phi(n^{-1/2}u)+n\phi(n^{-1/2}u_{+}))(1+O(n^{-1})).

It then follows from Equation
SA-12
that if we define
\mathsf{U}_{+}
to be a random variable with density

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{U}_{+}}(u)=\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta+v_{+}^{2}}{2\pi}}\exp(-(1-\beta+v_{+}^{2})(u-u_{+})^{2}/2),

then by Taylor expanding
\phi
at
v_{+}=n^{-1/2}u_{+}
and a similar argument as in the proof for high temperature case,

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{TV}}(\mathsf{U}_{n}|\mathsf{U}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{+},\mathsf{U}_{+})=O(n^{-1/2}).

The rest follows from the same argument as in the proof for high temperature case and is sub-Gaussianity of
\mathsf{U}_{n}
condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{\ell}
,
\ell\in\{-,+\}
.

SA-8.6
Proof for Remark
SA-1

Take
\mathsf{V}_{n}=n^{-1/2}\mathsf{U}_{n}
and
\mathsf{Z}
be a
\mathsf{N}(0,1)
variable independent to
\mathscr{m}
and
\mathsf{U}_{n}
. Based on the conditional mean and variance formulas in Equation (
SA-8
), using the conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
Berry-Esseen bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{m}<0|\mathsf{U}_{n}\geq 0)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{Z}+n^{\frac{1}{4}}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{U}_{n})<0|\mathsf{U}_{n}\geq 0)+O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Using the fact that
v(\mathsf{U}_{n})
is bounded above and
\mathsf{Z}
is Gaussian, and Taylor expanding
\tanh
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{m}<0|\mathsf{U}_{n}\geq 0)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\sqrt{\log n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\mathsf{U}_{n}\geq 0)+O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{U}_{n}\leq\sqrt{\log n}|\mathsf{U}_{n}\geq 0)+O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

The proof of Lemma
SA-3
(low temperature) shows that
d_{\operatorname{TV}}(\mathsf{U}_{n}|\mathsf{U}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{+},\mathsf{U}_{+})=O(n^{-1/2})
where
\mathsf{U}_{+}\sim\mathsf{N}(\sqrt{n}\pi_{+},(1-\beta(1-\pi_{+}^{2}))^{-1})
. Hence
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{U}_{n}\leq\sqrt{\log n}|\mathsf{U}_{n}\geq 0)\lesssim\exp(-n)
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{m}<0|\mathsf{U}_{n}\geq 0)=O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

By symmetry and the fact that
\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell)=\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{sgn}(\mathsf{U}_{n})=\ell)=1/2
for
\ell=-,+
, we know

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\{\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell\}\Delta\{\operatorname{sgn}(\mathsf{U}_{n})=\ell\})=O(n^{-1/2}),\qquad\ell=-,+.

The conclusion then follows from Lemma
SA-2
(3).

SA-8.7
Proof for Lemma
SA-4
Drifting from High Temperature

Throughout the proof, we denote by
\mathtt{C}
an absolute constant, and
\mathtt{K}
a constant that only depends on the distribution of
X_{i}
.

Let
\mathsf{U}_{n}(c)
,
e(\mathsf{U}_{n}(c))
,
v(\mathsf{U}_{n}(c))
be the latent variable, conditional mean, and conditional variance as previously defined when
\beta_{n}=1+cn^{-\frac{1}{2}}
,
c<0
. For notational simplicity, we abbreviate the
c
, and call them
\mathsf{U}_{n},e(\mathsf{U}_{n}),v(\mathsf{U}_{n})
respectively. By Lemma
SA-2
,
\lVert\mathsf{U}_{n}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\leq\mathtt{C}n^{1/4}
.

Step 1: Conditional Berry-Esseen.

Apply Berry-Esseen Theorem conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
in the same way as in the high temperature case, we get

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(\mathscr{g}_{n},v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}+\sqrt{n}e(\mathsf{U}_{n})\right)\leq\mathtt{K}n^{-1/2}.

Step 2: Non-Normal Approximation for
n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{U}_{n}
.

Consider
\mathsf{W}_{n}=n^{-1/4}\mathsf{U}_{n}
. Then
f_{\mathsf{W}_{n}}(w)=I_{n}(c)^{-1}h_{n}(w)
, with
I_{n}(c)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h_{n}(w)dw
, and

\displaystyle h_{n}(w)=\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}w^{2}+n\log\cosh\left(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\beta_{n}}w\right)\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{c}{2}w^{2}-\frac{\beta_{n}^{2}}{12}w^{4}+g(w)\beta_{n}^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{6}\right),

where by smoothness of
\log(\cosh(\cdot))
,
\lVert\theta\rVert_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{K}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{-\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}^{\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}h_{n}(w)dw

\displaystyle=\int_{-\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}^{\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}\exp(-\frac{c}{2}w^{2}-\frac{\beta_{n}^{2}}{12}w^{4})dw[1+O(\mathtt{C}^{6}(\log n)^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}})]

(SA-17)

\displaystyle=I(c)[1+O(\mathtt{C}^{6}(\log n)^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}})].

(SA-18)

Moreover, by a change of variable and the fact that
\beta_{n}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle I_{n}(c)

\displaystyle:=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h_{n}(w)dw=n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp\Big(-n\Big(\frac{v^{2}}{2}-\log\cosh(\sqrt{\beta_{n}v})\Big)\Big)dv

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp\Big(-n\Big(\frac{v^{2}}{2}-\log\cosh(\sqrt{v})\Big)\Big)dv\leq\mathtt{C}.

Since
\lVert\mathsf{W}_{n}(c)\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\leq\mathtt{C}
,
I_{n}(c)^{-1}\int_{(-\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n},\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n})^{c}}h_{n}(w)dw\leq\mathtt{C}n^{-1/2}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\int_{(-\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n},\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n})^{c}}h_{n}(w)dw\leq\mathtt{C}n^{-1/2}.

(SA-19)

Combining Equation
SA-17
and
SA-19
, we have
I_{n}(c)=I(c)[1+O(\mathtt{C}^{6}(\log n)^{3}n^{-1/2})]
. It follows that

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{TV}}(\mathsf{W}_{n},\mathsf{W})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{-\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}^{\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}\Big|\frac{h_{n}(w)}{I_{n}(c)}-\frac{h(w)}{I(c)}\Big|dw+\mathbb{P}(|\mathsf{W}_{n}|\geq\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n})+\mathbb{P}(|\mathsf{W}|\geq\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{-\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}^{\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}\Big|\frac{h_{n}(w)-h(w)}{I(c)}\Big|+h_{n}(w)\Big|\frac{1}{I(c)}-\frac{1}{I_{n}(c)}\Big|dw+O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{-\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}^{\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}\exp\Big(-\frac{c}{2}w^{2}-\frac{\beta_{n}^{2}}{12}w^{4}\Big)\frac{w^{6}}{\sqrt{n}I(c)}dw+\int_{-\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}^{\mathtt{C}\sqrt{\log n}}\frac{1}{I(c)}O(\mathtt{C}^{6}(\log n)^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}})dw+O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathtt{C}(\log n)^{3}n^{-1/2}.

Step 3: A Reduction through TV-distance Inequality.

Since
\mathsf{Z}\raisebox{0.50003pt}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{$\models$}}(\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathsf{W}_{n})
, we can use data processing inequality to get

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}v(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{Z}+n^{\frac{1}{4}}e(\mathsf{U}_{n}),n^{-\frac{1}{4}}v(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{Z}+n^{\frac{1}{4}}e(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W})\right)

\displaystyle\leq d_{\operatorname{TV}}\left(\mathsf{W}_{n},\mathsf{W}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathtt{C}(\log n)^{3}n^{-1/2}.

Step 4: Non-Gaussian Approximation for
n^{\frac{1}{4}}e(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W})
.

This is essentially the same as the proof for step 4 from the critical temperature case in Lemma
SA-3
.

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}\Big(n^{1/4}e(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W}),\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\mathsf{W}\Big)\leq\mathtt{K}\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}.

Step 5: Stabilization of Variance.

Using the same argument as Step 4 in the high temperature case for Lemma
SA-3
, and
\lVert\mathsf{W}\rVert\leq\mathtt{K}
,

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{KS}}(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}v(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{Z}+n^{\frac{1}{4}}e(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W}),n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{Z}+\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\mathsf{W}))\leq\mathtt{K}\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}.

The conclusion then follows from putting together the previous five steps.

SA-8.8
Proof for Lemma
SA-4
Drifting from Low Temperature

Consider the same
\mathsf{U}_{n}
defined in Equation (
SA-6
). Recall
\phi(v)=\frac{v^{2}}{2}-\log\cosh(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v)
,
\phi^{\prime}(v)=v-\sqrt{\beta_{n}}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v)
,
\phi^{(2)}(v)=1-\beta_{n}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v)
. And we take
v_{n,+}>0
,
v_{n,-}<0
to be the two solutions of
v-\sqrt{\beta_{n}}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v)=0
.

Step 2’: Non-Normal Approximation for
n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{U}_{n}
.

Take
\mathsf{V}_{n}=n^{-1/2}\mathsf{U}_{n}
. Then
f_{\mathsf{V}_{n}}(v)\propto\exp(-n\phi(v))
. Taylor expanding
\phi^{\prime}
at
0
, we know there exists some function
g
that is uniformly bounded such that
\phi^{\prime}(v)=(1-\beta_{n})v+\frac{1}{3}\beta_{n}^{2}v^{3}+\beta_{n}^{3}g(v)v^{5}
. Hence

v_{n,+}=\sqrt{\frac{3(\beta_{n}-1)}{\beta_{n}^{2}}}+O(\beta_{n}-1)=\sqrt{3c}n^{-1/4}+O(n^{-1/2}).

Taylor expand
\tanh
and
\sech
at
0
,

\displaystyle\phi^{(2)}(v_{n,+})

\displaystyle=1-\beta_{n}+v_{n,+}^{2}

\displaystyle=-cn^{-1/2}+3cn^{-1/2}(1+O(cn^{-1/2}))^{-2}+O((cn^{-1/2})^{5/2})

\displaystyle=2cn^{-1/2}(1+O(cn^{-1/2})),

\displaystyle\phi^{(3)}(v_{n,+})

\displaystyle=2(\beta_{n}-v_{n,+}^{2})v_{n,+}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\beta_{n}^{3/2}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{n,+})\tanh(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{n,+})

\displaystyle=2(1+O(cn^{-1/2}))(1+O(v_{n,+}^{2}))(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{n,+}+O(v_{n,+}^{3}))

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{3c}n^{-1/4}(1+O(cn^{-1/2})),

\displaystyle\phi^{(4)}(v_{n,+})

\displaystyle=2(\beta-v_{n,+}^{2})(\beta-3v_{n,+}^{2})

\displaystyle=2\beta_{n}^{2}\sech^{4}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{n,+})-4\beta_{n}^{2}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{n,+})\tanh^{2}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{n,+})

\displaystyle=2(1+O(cn^{-1/2})).

Take
\mathsf{W}_{n}=n^{1/4}\mathsf{V}_{n}=n^{-1/4}\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
\mathscr{w}_{+}=n^{1/4}v_{n,+}=\sqrt{3c}+O(n^{-1/4})
, and
\mathscr{w}_{-}=n^{1/4}v_{n,-}
. Define

\displaystyle h_{c,n}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{n}\phi^{(2)}(v_{n,+})}{2}(w-\mathscr{w}_{\operatorname{sgn}(w)})^{2}-\frac{n^{1/4}\phi^{(3)}(v_{n,+})}{6}(w-\mathscr{w}_{\operatorname{sgn}(w)})^{3}-\frac{\phi^{(4)}(v_{n,+})}{24}(w-\mathscr{w}_{\operatorname{sgn}(w)})^{4}.

By a change of variable and Taylor expansion, the density for
\mathsf{W}_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{W}_{n}}(w)\propto g_{c,\gamma}(w)=\exp\Big(h_{c,n}(w)+O(\lVert\phi^{(6)}\rVert_{\infty}/6!)\frac{(w-\mathscr{w}_{\operatorname{sgn}(w)})^{6}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big).

(SA-20)

By Lemma
SA-2
, for
\ell\in\{-,+\}
, condition on
\mathsf{W}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{c,n,\ell}
,
\mathsf{W}_{n}-\mathscr{w}_{\ell}
is sub-Gaussian with
\psi_{2}
-norm bounded by
\mathtt{C}
. Let
\mathsf{W}_{c,n}
be a random variable with density at
w
proportional to
\exp(h_{c,n}(w))
. By similar argument as Equations
SA-17
and
SA-19
,

d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathsf{W}_{n}|\mathsf{W}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{c,n,\ell},\mathsf{W}_{c,n}|\mathsf{W}_{c,n}\in\pazocal{I}_{c,n,\ell})\leq\mathtt{C}(\log n)^{3}n^{-1/2}).

The other steps,
conditional Berry-Esseen
,
reduction through TV-distance inequality
, and
non-Gaussian approximation for
n^{\frac{1}{4}}e(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W}_{c,n})
can be proceeded in the same way as in the proof for Lemma
SA-3
, with
\mathsf{W}_{n}-\mathscr{w}_{\ell}
sub-Gaussian condition on
\mathsf{W}_{n}\in\pazocal{I}_{c,n,\ell}
with
\psi_{2}
-norm bounded by
\mathtt{C}
, and respectively for
\mathsf{W}_{c,n}
.

SA-8.9
Proof for Lemma
SA-5
Knife-Edge Representation

Again we take
\mathsf{U}_{n}
to be the latent variable from Lemma
SA-1
, and
\mathsf{W}_{n}=n^{-1/4}\mathsf{U}_{n}
. From Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
SA-4
,
f_{\mathsf{W}_{n}}(w)=I_{n}(c)^{-1}h_{n}(w)
, with
I_{n}(c)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h_{n}(w)dw
, and

\displaystyle h_{n}(w)=\exp(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}w^{2}+n\log\cosh(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\beta_{n}}w))=\exp(-\frac{c_{n}}{2}w^{2}-\frac{\beta_{n}^{2}}{12}w^{4}+g(w)\beta_{n}^{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{6}),

where by smoothness of
\log(\cosh(\cdot))
,
\lVert\theta\rVert_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{K}
.

Case 1: When
\sqrt{n}(\beta_{n}-1)=o(1)
.
We can apply Berry-Esseen conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
the same way as in the proof of Lemma
SA-4
, and its Step 2 can also be applied here to show that if we take
\widetilde{\mathsf{W}}_{c}
to be a random variable with density proportional to
\exp(-c_{n}^{2}/2w^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}/12w^{4})
, then
d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathsf{W}_{n},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}}_{c})=O((\log n)^{3}n^{-1/2})
. Moreover,
c_{n}=o(1)
and
\beta_{n}=1-o(1)
. Hence
d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathsf{W}_{n},\mathsf{W}_{0})=o(1)
. The rest of the proof then follows from Step 3 to Step 5 in the proof for the critical regime case in Lemma
SA-3
.

Case 2: When
\sqrt{n}(1-\beta_{n})\gg 1
.
Again we still have
\lVert\mathsf{U}_{n}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}=O(n^{1/4})
. And we take
v_{+}>0
,
v_{-}<0
to be the two solutions of
v-\sqrt{\beta_{n}}\tanh(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v)=0
. Similarly as in the previous case, the first two steps in the proof of Lemma
SA-4
implies
d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathsf{W}_{n},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}}_{c})=o(1)
, where the density of
\mathsf{W}_{c}
is proportional to
\exp(-c_{n}^{2}/2w^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}/12w^{4})
. Since
c_{n}\gg 1
, the first term in the exponent dominates, and we can show
d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathsf{W}_{n},\mathsf{W}_{c}^{{\dagger}})=o(1)
, where
\mathsf{W}_{c}^{\dagger}
has density proportional to
\exp(-c_{n}^{2}/2w^{2})
. Again, we can Taylor expand to get
n^{1/4}e(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W}))=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]n^{\frac{1}{4}}\tanh\left(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathsf{W}\right)=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}][\mathsf{W}-O(\frac{\mathsf{W}^{2}}{3\sqrt{n}})]
, and show
d_{\operatorname{KS}}(n^{1/4}e(n^{1/4}\mathsf{W}_{c}^{\dagger}),\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\mathsf{W}_{c}^{\dagger})=o(1)
. Combining with stablization of variance as in the proof of Lemma
SA-8
(high temperature case), we can show

d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathscr{g}_{n},n^{-1/4}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}+\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\mathsf{W}_{c}^{\dagger})=o(1).

Since
\mathsf{Z}
and
\mathsf{W}_{c}^{\dagger}
are independent Gaussian random variables, we also have
d_{\operatorname{KS}}(\mathscr{g}_{n}/\sqrt{\mathbb{V}[\mathscr{g}_{n}]},\mathsf{Z})=o(1)
.

Case 3: When
\sqrt{n}(\beta_{n}-1)\gg 1
.
By Lemma
SA-4
(2),

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}_{\beta_{n},h}(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathscr{g}_{n}\leq t|\mathscr{m}\in\pazocal{I}_{c,\ell})-\mathbb{P}(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{Z}+\beta_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\mathsf{W}_{c_{n},n}\leq t

\displaystyle|\mathsf{W}_{c_{n},n}\in\pazocal{I}_{c,\ell})\bigg|=o(1),

(SA-21)

where
\mathsf{W}_{c,n}
has density proportional to
\exp(h_{c,n}(w))
, with

\displaystyle h_{c,n}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{n}\phi^{(2)}(v_{+})}{2}(w-\mathscr{w}_{\operatorname{sgn}(w)})^{2}-\frac{n^{1/4}\phi^{(3)}(v_{+})}{6}(w-\mathscr{w}_{\operatorname{sgn}(w)})^{3}-\frac{\phi^{(4)}(v_{+})}{24}(w-\mathscr{w}_{\operatorname{sgn}(w)})^{4},

and
\pazocal{I}_{c,n,-}=(-\infty,K_{c,n,-})
and
\pazocal{I}_{c,n,+}=(K_{c,n,+},\infty)
such that
\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{W}_{c,n}|\mathsf{W}_{c,n}\in\pazocal{I}_{c,n,\ell}]=w_{c,n,\ell}
for
\ell\in\{-,+\}
. Now we calculate the order of the coefficients under
\sqrt{n}(\beta_{n}-1)\gg 1
. First, suppose
\beta_{n}=1+cn^{\gamma}
for some
\gamma\in(0,\infty)
and
c
not depending on
n
. Then
v_{+}=\sqrt{\frac{3(\beta_{n}-1)}{\beta_{n}^{2}}}+O(\beta_{n}-1)=\sqrt{3c}n^{-\gamma/2}+O(n^{-\gamma})
. Taylor expand
\tanh
and
\sech
at
0
,

\displaystyle\phi^{(2)}(v_{+})

\displaystyle=1-\beta_{n}+v_{+}^{2}=-cn^{-\gamma}+cn^{-\gamma}3(1+cn^{-\gamma})^{-2}+O((cn^{-\gamma})^{5/2})

\displaystyle=2cn^{-\gamma}(1+O(cn^{-\gamma})),

\displaystyle\phi^{(3)}(v_{+})

\displaystyle=2\beta_{n}^{3/2}\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{+})\tanh(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{+})

\displaystyle=2(1+O(cn^{-\gamma}))(1+O(v_{+}^{2}))(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v_{+}+O(v_{+}^{3}))

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{3c}n^{-\gamma/2}(1+O(cn^{-\gamma})),

\displaystyle\phi^{(4)}(v_{+})

\displaystyle=-2\beta_{n}^{4}\sech^{4}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v)+4\sech^{2}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v)\tanh^{2}(\sqrt{\beta_{n}}v)

\displaystyle=-2(1+O(cn^{-\gamma})).

We see when
\gamma=1/2
, all of
\sqrt{n}\phi^{(2)}(v_{+})
,
n^{1/4}\phi^{(3)}(v_{+})
and
\phi^{(4)}(v_{+})
are of order 1. And when
c_{n}=\sqrt{n}(\beta_{n}-1)\gg 1
, we have
\sqrt{n}\phi^{(2)}(v_{+})\gg n^{1/4}\phi^{(3)}(v_{+})\gg\phi^{(4)}(v_{+})
. Since
w_{+}=n^{1/4}v_{+}=\sqrt{3c_{n}}\gg 1
, and similarly,
|w_{-}|\gg 1
, condition on
\mathsf{W}_{c,n}\in[n]
,
\mathsf{W}_{c,n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{W}_{c,n}|\mathsf{W}_{c,n}\in[n]]
is
\mathtt{C}
-sub-Gaussian,
\ell\in\{-,+\}
. By similar concentration arguments as in the proof for Step 2 in Lemma
SA-4
(1), we can show the second order term in
h_{c,n}
dominates, and for
\ell\in\{-,+\}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{P}_{\beta_{n},h}(\mathsf{W}_{c,n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{W}_{c,n}|\mathsf{W}_{c,n}\in\pazocal{I}_{\ell}]\leq t|\mathsf{W}_{c,n}\in\pazocal{I}_{\ell})-\Phi(\sqrt{n(1-\beta_{n}+v_{\ell}^{2})}t)|=o(1).

The conclusion then follows from pluggin the (conditional) Gaussian approximation for
\mathsf{W}_{c_{n},n}
back into Equation (
SA-21
), and the fact that
\mathsf{Z}
is independent to
\mathsf{W}_{c,n}
and also Gaussian.

SA-8.10
Proof of Lemma
SA-6

Throughout the proof, the Ising spins
\mathbf{W}=(W_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
are distributed according to Assumption
SA-1
with parameters
(\beta,h)
. For brevity, we write
\mathbb{P}
in place of
\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}
.

I. High Temperature or Nonzero External Field

Let
\mathsf{U}_{n}
be the latent random variable from Lemma
SA-2
. Condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
\mathbf{X}_{i}W_{i}
’s are i.i.d random vectors. For
u\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\displaystyle\Sigma(u)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Cov}[\mathbf{X}_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}]\mathbb{E}[(W_{i}-\pi)^{2}|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u]-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi)^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\operatorname{Cov}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\mathbb{E}[(W_{i}-\pi)^{2}|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u]

\displaystyle\gtrsim\operatorname{Cov}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\min\{(1-\pi)^{2},(1+\pi)^{2}\},

\displaystyle e(u)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}](\tanh(\sqrt{\beta/n}u+h)-\pi),

and to save notations, we denote

\displaystyle t(u)=\sqrt{n}(\tanh(\sqrt{\beta/n}u+h)-\pi).

Suppose
\mathsf{Z}_{d}\sim\mathsf{N}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{d\times d})
independent to
\mathsf{U}_{n}
. By
(
4
, Theorem 2.1)

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{A\in\mathscr{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)\in A\bigg|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u\bigg)-\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Sigma(u)^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\in A\bigg)\bigg|

(SA-22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{B_{n}^{2}\log(n)^{7}}{n}\Big)^{1/6}.

From the proofs of Lemma
SA-3
, we know the term
t(\mathsf{U}_{n})
stabilizes,

d_{\operatorname{KS}}\Big(t(\mathsf{U}_{n}),\sigma\mathsf{Z}\Big)=O(n^{-1/2}),\qquad\sigma=\Big(\frac{\beta(1-\pi^{2})^{2}}{1-\beta(1-\pi^{2})}\Big)^{1/2}.

By Lemma
SA-2
,

\displaystyle\lVert\Sigma(\mathsf{U}_{n})-\Sigma\rVert=O_{\psi,2}(n^{-1/2}),\qquad\Sigma=(1-\pi^{2})\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}].

For each
\varepsilon
, define
A_{\varepsilon}
to be the event
\{\lVert\Sigma(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}-\Sigma^{1/2})\mathsf{Z}_{d}\rVert\leq\varepsilon\}
. Since
d
is fixed, we can work with each dimension to get

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\mathbb{P}(\Sigma(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\mathbf{t})-\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\mathbf{t})|

(SA-23)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\sup_{\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\mathbb{P}(\Sigma(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\mathbf{t},A_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\mathbf{t},A_{\varepsilon})|+\mathbb{P}(A_{\varepsilon}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>0}2\mathbb{P}(A_{\varepsilon}^{c})+\sup_{\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\sup_{\bm{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d},\lVert\bm{\varepsilon}\rVert\leq\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}\in(\mathbf{t}-\bm{\varepsilon},\mathbf{t}+\bm{\varepsilon}))

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\exp(-n\varepsilon^{2})+\sup_{\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d},}\sup_{\bm{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d},\lVert\bm{\varepsilon}\rVert\leq\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}\in(\mathbf{t}-\bm{\varepsilon},\mathbf{t}+\bm{\varepsilon}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log n}),

where in the last line, we have chosen
\varepsilon=n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log n}
and used Nazarov’s inequality (Lemma A.1 in
[
4
]
). Since
\mathsf{Z}_{d}
and
\mathsf{U}_{n}
are independent, we can show via data processing inequality that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n}))\leq\mathbf{t})-\mathbb{P}((\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d})+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\sigma\mathsf{Z})\leq\mathbf{t})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\mathbb{P}(t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\mathbf{t})-\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\sigma\mathsf{Z}\leq\mathbf{t})|=O(n^{-1/2}).

Combining the previous results,

\displaystyle\sup_{A\in\mathscr{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}W_{i}\in A\bigg)-\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\sigma\mathsf{Z}_{1}\in A)\bigg|=O(n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log n}).

II. Critical Temperature

We still have conditional Berry-Esseen as in Equation (
SA-22
). The proof of Lemma
SA-3
implies

d_{\operatorname{KS}}(n^{-1/4}t(\mathsf{U}_{n}),\mathsf{R})=O(n^{-1/2}).

Hence
\lVert\Sigma(\mathsf{U}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[\Sigma(\mathsf{U}_{n})]\rVert_{\operatorname{max}}=O_{\psi,2}(n^{-1/4})
. By concentration of
\mathsf{U}_{n}
, approximation of
n^{-1/4}t(\mathsf{U}_{n})
by
\mathsf{R}
, and anti-concentration of
\mathsf{R}
, we can use similar arguments as Equation (
SA-23
) to get

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\mathbb{P}(\Sigma(\mathsf{U}_{n})^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\mathbf{t})-\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\mathbf{t})|=O(n^{-1/2}(\log n)^{1/4}).

By independence between
\mathsf{Z}_{d}
and
\mathsf{U}_{n}
, and approximation of
n^{-1/4}t(\mathsf{U}_{n})
by
\mathsf{R}
, we can use data processing inequality to get

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\mathbb{P}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]t(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq\mathbf{t})-\mathbb{P}(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\mathsf{R})|=O(n^{-1/2}).

Figure SA-1
:
The law
\mathbb{P}
of
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]W
, concentrates on
\mathscr{C}=\{s\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]:s\in\mathbb{R}\}
, while the law
\mathbb{Q}
of
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\mathsf{W}+n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathsf{Z}_{d}
is degenerate. The bulk mass of
\mathbb{Q}
lies in a cylinder with axis
\mathscr{C}
and width of order
\sqrt{\log n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}
. Consider
\Sigma=I_{2}
,
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]=\mathbf{e}_{2}
,
\mathscr{C}=\{s\mathbf{e}_{2}:s\in\mathbb{R}\}
, and
A=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:-M\leq x\leq-\varepsilon,-M\leq y\leq M\}
for some small
\varepsilon>0
and large
M>0
. Then
\mathbb{P}(A)=0
while
\mathbb{Q}(A)
is close to
\frac{1}{2}
.

It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{A\in\mathscr{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg(n^{-1/4}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}W_{i}\in A\bigg)-\mathbb{P}(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{d}+\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}]\mathsf{R}\in A)\bigg|=O(n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log n}).

III. Low Temperature

We still have conditional Berry-Esseen as in Equation (
SA-22
). From the proof of Lemma
SA-3
(3) and Remark
SA-1
, for
\ell=-,+
,

d_{\operatorname{KS}}(t(\mathsf{U}_{n})-\sqrt{n}\pi_{\ell}|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell,\sigma\mathsf{Z})=O(n^{-1/2}),

where
\sigma^{2}=\frac{\beta(1-\pi_{+}^{2})^{2}}{1-\beta(1-\pi_{+}^{2})}
. The rest of the proof follows from the arguments for
I. High Temperature or Nonzero External Field
, using conditional concentration of
\mathsf{U}_{n}
given
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})
.

SA-9
Proofs: Section
SA-3

SA-9.1
Proof of Lemma
SA-7

Our proof is constructive. We show that consistent estimate of
n\mathbb{V}[\widehat{\tau}_{n}]
would imply that one can distinguish between two constructed hypotheses easily. Let
\mathscr{P}_{n}
be the class of distributions of random vectors
(\mathbf{W}=(W_{1},\cdots,W_{n}),\mathbf{Y}=(Y_{1},\cdots,Y_{n}))
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
that satisfies Assumptions 1,2,3. Consider the following two data generating processes:

\displaystyle\text{DGP}_{0}:

\displaystyle\quad\beta=0,\quad G(\cdot,\cdot)\equiv 1,\quad\rho_{n}=1,\quad Y_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)=f_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)+\varepsilon_{i},\quad f_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)\equiv 1,

\displaystyle\text{DGP}_{1}:

\displaystyle\quad\beta=u,\quad G(\cdot,\cdot)\equiv 1,\quad\rho_{n}=1,\quad Y_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)=f_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)+\varepsilon_{i},\quad f_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)\equiv 1,

where
0<u<1
, and in both cases
(\varepsilon_{i}:1\leq i\leq n)
are i.i.d
\mathsf{N}(0,1)
random variables, independent to
\mathbf{W}
. Denote by
\mathbb{P}_{0,n}
and
\mathbb{P}_{1,n}
the laws of
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y})
under
\text{DGP}_{0}
and
\text{DGP}_{1}
. Then

\displaystyle d_{\text{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y}))

\displaystyle=d_{\text{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{W}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{W}))+d_{\text{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{Y}|\mathbf{W}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{Y}|\mathbf{W}))

\displaystyle=d_{\text{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{W}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{W})),

the first line uses chain rule of
d_{\text{KL}}
, the second line uses

d_{\text{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{Y}|\mathbf{W}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{Y}|\mathbf{W}))=d_{\text{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{Y}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{Y}))=0.

From Theorem 2.3 (and its proof) in
[
1
]
,

M:=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}d_{\text{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{W}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{W}))<\infty.

Hence for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle d_{\text{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y}))

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{1}{2}\exp(-d_{\text{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y})))

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{1}{2}\exp(-M).

Le Cam’s method (Section 15.2.1 in
[
13
]
) gives for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}}}\sup_{\mathbb{P}_{n}\in\mathscr{P}_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}[n(\widehat{\mathbb{V}}[\widehat{\tau}-\tau]-\mathbb{V}[\widehat{\tau}-\tau])]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle n|\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{P}_{n,0}}[\widehat{\tau}-\tau]-\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{P}_{n,1}}[\widehat{\tau}-\tau]|(1-d_{\text{TV}}(\mathbb{P}_{0,n}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y}),\mathbb{P}_{1,n}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Y})))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varepsilon\exp(-M)/2,

in the last line we used Theorem 2 (1) to get
n\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{P}_{n,0}}[\widehat{\tau}-\tau]-n\mathbb{V}_{\mathbb{P}_{n,1}}[\widehat{\tau}-\tau]=\varepsilon(1+o(1))
.

SA-9.2
Proof of Lemma
SA-8

The following discussions will be organized according to the three different cases: (1) When
\beta<1
. (2) When
\beta\geq 1
,
\mathscr{m}
concentrates around
0
. (3) When
\beta\geq 1
and
\mathscr{m}
concentrates around two symmetric locations
w_{+}>0
and
w_{-}<0
with
|w_{+}|=|w_{-}|
.

We have required
\widehat{\beta}\in[0,1]
. For analysis, consider an unrestricted pseud-likelihood estimator,

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}=\argmax_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}}l(\beta;\mathbf{W}),

where
l(\beta;\mathbf{W})
is the pseudo log-likelihood given by

\displaystyle l(\beta;\mathbf{W})=\sum_{i\in[n]}\log\mathbb{P}_{\beta}\left(W_{i}\mid\mathbf{W}_{-i}\right)=\sum_{i\in[n]}-\log\bigg(\frac{1}{2}W_{i}\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}_{i})+\frac{1}{2}\bigg).

We show that
l(\beta;\mathbf{W})
is concave.

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}l(\beta;\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(n^{-1}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j})W_{i}\sech^{2}(\beta n^{-1}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j})}{W_{i}\tanh(\beta n^{-1}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j})+1}
