\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bigg(}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}{\bigg)}(W_{i}-\tanh(\beta n^{-1}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j})),

and

\displaystyle l^{(2)}(\beta;\mathbf{W})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bigg(}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}{\bigg)}^{2}\sech^{2}{\bigg(}\frac{\beta}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}{\bigg)}>0.

Hence
l(\cdot;\mathbf{W})
is concave everywhere in
\mathbb{R}
. This shows
\widehat{\beta}=\min\{\max\{\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}},0\},1\}
. Now we study limiting distribution of
\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}

1. High and critical temperature regime.

To obtain a more precise distribution for
\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}
, we use Fermat’s condition to obtain that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bigg(}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}{\bigg)}{\bigg(}W_{i}-\tanh{\bigg(}\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}n^{-1}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}{\bigg)}{\bigg)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bigg(}\mathscr{m}-\frac{W_{i}}{n}{\bigg)}{\bigg(}W_{i}-\tanh(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}\mathscr{m})+\sech^{2}(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}\mathscr{m})\frac{\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}W_{i}}{n}+O(n^{-2}){\bigg)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bigg(}\mathscr{m}-\frac{W_{i}}{n}{\bigg)}{\bigg(}{\bigg(}1+\sech^{2}(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}\mathscr{m})\frac{\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}}{n}{\bigg)}W_{i}-\tanh(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}\mathscr{m})+O(n^{-2}){\bigg)}

\displaystyle={\bigg(}1+\frac{\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}}{n}\sech^{2}(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}\mathscr{m}){\bigg)}{\bigg(}\mathscr{m}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}{\bigg)}-\frac{n-1}{n}\mathscr{m}\tanh(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}\mathscr{m})+O(n^{-2})\mathscr{m},

here
O(\cdot)
’s are all up to an absolute constant. By Lemma
SA-4
with
X_{i}=1
, we can show
\mathbb{E}[|(n\mathscr{m})^{-1}|]\leq\mathtt{C}n^{-1/2}
. By Markov inequality,
(n\mathscr{m})^{-1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
. Taylor expanding
\tanh
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{(n-1)\mathscr{m}}\tanh^{-1}\left(\mathscr{m}-\frac{1}{n\mathscr{m}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{(n-1)\mathscr{m}}{\bigg(}\mathscr{m}-\frac{1}{n\mathscr{m}}+\frac{1}{3}{\bigg(}\mathscr{m}-\frac{1}{n\mathscr{m}}{\bigg)}^{3}+O{\bigg(}{\bigg(}\mathscr{m}-\frac{1}{n\mathscr{m}}{\bigg)}^{5}{\bigg)}{\bigg)}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{n\mathscr{m}^{2}}+\frac{\mathscr{m}^{2}}{3}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

(SA-24)

where in the above equation, both
O(\cdot)
and
O_{\mathbb{P}}(\cdot)
are up to absolute constants.
The rest of the results are given according to the different temperature regimes.

(1) The High Temperature Regime.
Using Lemma
SA-8
with
X_{i}=1
, our result for the high temperature regime with
\beta<1
implies that

n^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{m}\overset{d}{\to}\mathsf{N}(0,\frac{1}{1-\beta})\Rightarrow(1-\beta)n\mathscr{m}^{2}\overset{d}{\to}\chi^{2}(1)
. Therefore we conclude that
\frac{1-\beta}{1-\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}}\overset{d}{\to}\chi^{2}(1)
. The conclusion then follows from
\widehat{\beta}=\min\{\max\{\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}},0\},1\}
.

(2) The Critical Temperature Regime.

Using Lemma
SA-8
with
X_{i}=1
, we have
d_{\operatorname{KS}}(n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathscr{m},\mathsf{W}_{0})=o(1)
. This implies
n^{\frac{1}{2}}(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}-1)\overset{d}{\to}Law(\frac{\mathsf{W}_{0}^{2}}{3}-\frac{1}{\mathsf{W}_{0}^{2}}).
Since
\mathsf{W}_{0}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}<0)=o(1)
. The conclusion then follows from
\widehat{\beta}=\min\{\max\{\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}},0\},1\}
.

2. The low temperature regime.

When
\mathscr{m}
concentrates around
\pi_{+}
and
\pi_{-}
we have when
\mathscr{m}>0
, use the fact that
\pi_{\ell}=\tanh(\beta\pi_{\ell})
for
\ell\in\{+,-\}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}

\displaystyle-\beta=\frac{(1-O(n^{-1}))(\mathscr{m}-\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}))}{\mathscr{m}\sech^{2}(\beta\mathscr{m})}+\mathscr{m}O(\delta^{2})+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-O(n^{-1}))\left((\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})-(\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m})-\tanh(\beta\pi_{\ell}))\right)}{\pi_{\ell}\left(\sech^{2}(\beta\pi_{\ell})-2(\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})\tanh(\beta\pi_{\ell})\sech^{2}(\beta\pi_{\ell})+O(\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})^{2}\right)\left(1+\frac{\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell}}{\pi_{\ell}}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{m}O(\delta^{2})+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle=(1-O(n^{-1}))\frac{(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\beta\pi_{\ell}))(\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})}{\pi_{\ell}\sech^{2}(\beta\pi_{\ell})}(1+O(\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell}))+\mathscr{m}O(\delta^{2})+O(n^{-1}).

and the similar argument gives

\displaystyle\mathscr{m}(\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}-\beta^{\ast})=\frac{1-\beta^{\ast}\sech^{2}(\beta^{\ast}\pi_{\ell})}{\sech^{2}(\beta^{\ast}\pi_{\ell})}(\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})+O_{\psi_{1}}(n^{-1}).

The conclusion then Lemma
SA-3
(3) and the convergence of
\mathscr{m}
to
\pi_{+}
or
\pi_{-}
.

SA-9.3
Proof of Lemma
SA-9

Again we consider the unrestricted PMLE given by

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{\text{UR}}=\argmax_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}}l(\beta;\mathbf{W}),

where
l(\beta;\mathbf{W})
is the pseudo log-likelihood given by

\displaystyle l(\beta;\mathbf{W})=\sum_{i\in[n]}\log\mathbb{P}_{\beta}\left(W_{i}\mid\mathbf{W}_{-i}\right)=\sum_{i\in[n]}-\log\bigg(\frac{1}{2}W_{i}\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}_{i})+\frac{1}{2}\bigg).

For
\beta\in[0,1]
, that is
c_{\beta}=\sqrt{n}(\beta-1)\leq 0
, Equation (
SA-24
) and the approximation of
\mathscr{m}
by
n^{-1/2}\mathsf{Z}+n^{-1/4}\mathsf{W}_{c}
from Lemma
SA-4
gives

\displaystyle\sup_{\beta\in[0,1]}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{P}(1-\widehat{\beta}\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(z_{\beta,n}^{-2}-\frac{3}{n}z_{\beta,n}^{2}\leq t)|=o(1).

The conclusion follows from the fact that
x\mapsto\max\{\min\{x,0\},1\}
is
1
-Lipschitz.

SA-10
Proofs: Section
SA-4

SA-10.1
Preliminary Lemmas

Lemma SA-26
.

Recall
\mathbf{W}=(W_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}
takes value in
\{-1,1\}^{n}
with

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{w}\right)=\frac{1}{Z}\exp\bigg(\frac{\beta}{n}\sum_{i<j}W_{i}W_{j}+h\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\bigg),\quad h\neq 0\text{ or }h=0,0\leq\beta\leq 1.

Recall
\pi
is the unique solution to
x=\tanh(\beta x+h)
. Then
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}]=\pi+O(n^{-1})
.

Proof.

If
h=0
, then
\pi=\mathbb{E}[W_{i}]=0
. If
h\neq 0
, then the concentration of
\mathscr{m}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}
towards
\pi
in Lemma
SA-3
implies,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{i}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|W_{-i}]]=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}+h)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\beta\pi+h)+\sech^{2}(\beta\pi+h)(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi)-\sech^{2}(\beta m^{\ast}+h)\tanh(\beta m^{\ast}+h)(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi)^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tanh(\beta\pi+h)+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi+O(n^{-1}),

where
\mathscr{m}^{\ast}
is a number between
\mathscr{m}
and
\pi
, and we have used boundedness of
\sech
.
∎

Lemma SA-27
.

Suppose Assumption
SA-1
, 2, and 3 hold with
h=0,0\leq\beta\leq 1
or
h\neq 0
: (1)

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|=O_{\psi_{\beta,\gamma}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})+O_{\psi_{2}}(N_{i}^{-1/2}).

(2) Define
A(\mathbf{U})=(G(U_{i},U_{j}))_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
. Condition on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}=\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}:\min_{i\in[n]}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij}\geq 32\log n\}
, for large enough
n
, for each
i\in[n]
and
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|\geq 4\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}t^{1/2}+C_{\beta,h}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}t^{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}\middle|\mathbf{U}\right)\leq 2\exp(-t)+n^{-98},

where
C_{\beta,h}
is some constant that only depends on
\beta,h
.

(3) When
h=0
, and
\beta\in[0,1]
, then there exists a constant
\mathtt{K}
that does not depend on
\beta
, such that for large enough
n
, for each
i\in[n]
and
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|\geq 4\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}t^{1/2}+\mathtt{K}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}t\middle|\mathbf{U}\right)\leq 2\exp(-t)+n^{-98}.

Proof.

Take
\mathsf{U}_{n}
to be a random variable with density

\displaystyle f_{\mathsf{U}_{n}}(u)=\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+n\log\cosh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}u+h\right)\right)}{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}v^{2}+n\log\cosh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}v+h\right)\right)dv}.

Condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
W_{i}
’s are i.i.d. Decompose by

\displaystyle\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi=\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\left(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)+\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi.

Condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
W_{i}
’s are i.i.d. Berry-Esseen theorem condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
and
\mathbf{E}
gives,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\leq t\Big|\mathbf{E}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{v(\mathsf{U}_{n})}{N_{i}}}Z+e(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq t\Big|\mathbf{E}\Big)\bigg|=O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

(SA-25)

where
e(\mathsf{U}_{n}):=\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi=\tanh(\sqrt{\beta/n}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h)-\pi
, and
v(\mathsf{U}_{n}):=\mathbb{V}[W_{i}-\pi|\mathsf{U}_{n}]
. By McDiarmid’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(|\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\left(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)|\geq 2N_{i}^{-1/2}t\bigg|\mathbf{E}\bigg)\leq 2\exp(-t^{2}).

Plugging into Equation (
SA-25
), we can show (1) holds.

Next, we want to show condition on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,
\mathbb{P}(N_{i}\leq\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]/3|\mathbf{U})\leq n^{-100}
:

Notice that for any
\mathbf{U}
such that
\rho_{n}\min_{i\in[n]}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij}(\mathbf{U})\rightarrow\infty
,
Condition on
A
such that
A\in\mathscr{A}
,
E_{ij}=\rho A_{ij}\iota_{ij}
,
1\leq i\leq j\leq n
are i.i.d Bernouli random variables, and for each
i,j
,
\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ki}\geq 32\log n-1\geq 31\log n
for
n\geq 3
. By bounded difference inequality, for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|\sum_{k\neq i,j}E_{ki}-\sum_{k\neq i,j}\rho_{n}A_{ki}\bigg|\geq\rho_{n}\sqrt{\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{i,j}^{2}}t\bigg)\leq 2\exp(-2t^{2}).

Hence condition on
A
, with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq i,j}E_{ki}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k\neq i,j}\rho_{n}A_{ki}-8\sqrt{\log n}\rho_{n}\sqrt{\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ij}^{2}}\geq\rho_{n}\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ki}-8\sqrt{\log n}\rho_{n}\sqrt{\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ki}}

\displaystyle\geq\rho_{n}\sqrt{\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ki}}\left(\sqrt{\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ki}}-8\sqrt{\log n}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\rho_{n}\sqrt{\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ki}}\left(\sqrt{\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ki}}-8\sqrt{31^{-1}\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ij}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\rho_{n}\sum_{k\neq i,j}A_{ij}/3\geq\frac{31}{3}\log n,

(SA-26)

and since
\rho_{n}A_{i,j}=\mathbb{E}[E_{ij}|\mathbf{U}]\in[0,1]
,
\sum_{k\neq i,j}E_{ki}+1\geq\mathbb{E}[N_{j}|\mathbf{A}]/3
. By Equation
SA-26
, condition on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,
\mathbb{P}(N_{i}\leq\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]/3|\mathbf{U})\leq n^{-100}
.

Hence we can disintegrate over the distribution of
\mathbf{E}
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\left(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)|\geq 4\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}t\middle|\mathbf{U}\right)\leq 2\exp(-t^{2})+n^{-100}.

By Equation
SA-8
and Lemma
SA-2
, and the Lipschitzness of
\tanh
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}\left(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}\right).

Plugging into Equation (
SA-25
), we can show (2) holds.

Under the setting of (3), the only part that depends on
\beta
in our proof is
\mathsf{U}_{n}
. Since we show in Lemma
SA-2

\lVert\mathsf{U}_{n}\rVert_{\psi_{1}}\leq\mathtt{K}n^{1/4}
for some absolute constant
\mathtt{K}
, which is essentially the
\beta=1
rate, the conclusion of (3) then follows.
∎

SA-10.2
Proof of Lemma
SA-10

Since we use the conditional probability
p_{i}
in the inverse probability weight, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{n,\text{UB}}|(f_{i})_{i\in[n]},\mathbf{E}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{p_{i}}-\frac{(1-T_{i})Y_{i}}{1-p_{i}}\bigg|(f_{i})_{i\in[n]},\mathbf{E}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{p_{i}}-\frac{(1-T_{i})Y_{i}}{1-p_{i}}\bigg|\mathbf{T}_{-i},(f_{i})_{i\in[n]},\mathbf{E}\bigg]\bigg|(f_{i})_{i\in[n]},\mathbf{E}\bigg],

and the conclusion follows from
\mathbb{E}[T_{i}|\mathbf{T}_{-i},(f_{i})_{i\in[n]},\mathbf{E}]=p_{i}
.

SA-10.3
Proof of Lemma
SA-11

First consider the treatment part.

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)+n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right).

For the second term, taylor expand
p_{i}^{-1},p_{i}
as follows:

\displaystyle p_{i}^{-1}=

\displaystyle 1+\exp\left(-2\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}-2h\right)=1+\exp\left(-2\beta\frac{n-1}{n}\pi-2h\right)

(SA-27)

\displaystyle-\exp\left(-2\beta\frac{n-1}{n}\pi-2h\right)2\beta\left(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\frac{n-1}{n}\pi\right)+\frac{1}{2}\exp(-\xi_{i}^{\ast})4\beta^{2}\left(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\frac{n-1}{n}\pi\right)^{2},

where
\xi_{i}^{\ast}
is some random quantity that lies between
4\frac{\beta}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}
and
4\frac{\beta}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}\pi
. Taking the parameters
c_{i}^{+}=g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)\left(1+\exp(-2\beta\pi-2h)\right)
,
d^{+}=\beta(1-\tanh(\beta\pi+h))\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\pi)].
Then

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(T_{i}-p_{i})g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)(1+\exp\left(-2\beta\pi-2h\right)-\exp\left(-2\beta\pi-2h\right)2\beta(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi))

\displaystyle\qquad+O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{i}(T_{i}-p_{i})+O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}((\log n)^{1/2}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{i}^{+}\left[T_{i}-\frac{1}{1+\exp(-2\beta\pi-2h)}-\frac{2\beta\exp(2\beta\pi+2h)}{(1+\exp(2\beta\pi+2h))^{2}}(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}((\log n)^{1/2}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{c_{i}^{+}}{2}\left(W_{i}-\tanh(\beta\pi+h)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\beta\exp(2\beta\pi+2h)}{(1+\exp(2\beta\pi+2h))^{2}}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}c_{j}^{+})\left(W_{i}-\pi\right)+O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}((\log n)^{1/2}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)+(c_{i}^{+}/2-d^{+})\left(W_{i}-\pi\right)\right]+O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}((\log n)^{1/2}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

Proof of (1):

By Lemma
SA-3
,
\mathscr{m}-\pi=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})
. The claim follows from Equation
SA-27
and a union bound argument.

Proof of (2):

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(T_{i}-p_{i})g_{i}(1,\pi)(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\mathscr{m}-\pi)n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(W_{i}-\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}+h))g_{i}(1,\pi)

\displaystyle\qquad+O(n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}).

By Lemma
SA-3
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{m}-\pi=O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

Taylor expand
\tanh(x)
at
x=\beta\pi+h
, we have

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}(1,\pi)(W_{i}-\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}+h))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}(1,\pi)(W_{i}-\tanh(\beta\pi+h)-\beta\sech^{2}(\beta\pi+h)(\mathscr{m}-\pi)+\tanh(\beta\pi+h)\sech^{2}(\beta\pi+h)(\mathscr{m}-\pi)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+O((\mathscr{m}-\pi)^{3}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\psi_{\beta,h,tc}}(1).

hence

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(T_{i}-p_{i})g_{i}(1,\pi)(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi)=O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}((\log n)^{1/2}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

Proof of (3):

The first line follows from a Taylor expansion of
p_{i}=(1+\exp(2\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}+2h))^{-1}
at
\pi
, and
\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})
, noticing that
c_{i}
,
\lVert\psi^{\prime\prime}\rVert_{\infty}
are bounded. The second line follows by reordering the terms.

Proof of (4):

By Lemma
SA-26
,
\tanh(\beta\pi+h)=\pi+O(n^{-1})
. By boundedness and i.i.d of
g_{i}(1,\pi)
,
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}c_{j}=\overline{c}+O(n^{-1})=\mathbb{E}[c_{i}]+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})+O(n^{-1})
. Similarly, for the control part, taking the parameters
c_{i}^{-}=g_{i}\left(-1,\pi\right)\left(1+\exp(2\beta\pi+2h)\right)
,
d^{-}=\beta(1-\tanh(-\beta\pi-h))\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(-1,\pi)].

\displaystyle-n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-T_{i}}{1-p_{i}}g_{i}(-1,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}(-1,\pi)+n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(c_{i}^{-}/2-d^{-})(W_{i}-\pi)+O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}((\log n)^{1/2}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

Using Lemma
SA-26
again, we can show
(1+\exp(-2\beta\pi-2h))/2=1/\pi+O(n^{-1})
and
(1+\exp(2\beta\pi+2h))/2=1/(1-\pi)+O(n^{-1})
,
\tanh(-\beta\pi-h)=-\pi+O(n^{-1})
. The result then follows from replacing these quantities in
c_{i}^{+},c_{i}^{-},d^{+},d^{-}
by corresponding ones using
\pi
.

SA-10.4
Proof of Lemma
SA-12

We decompose by
\Delta_{2,2}=\Delta_{2,2,1}+\Delta_{2,2,2}
, where

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1}=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}-\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]}{\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,2}=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}\left(p_{i}^{-1}-\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]^{-1}\right)g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right).

Notice that the first term is a quadractic form. Define
\mathbf{H}
such that
H_{ij}=\frac{g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)E_{ij}}{2\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]N_{i}}
. Then
\Delta_{2,2,1}=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}(\mathbf{W}-\pi)^{\operatorname{T}}\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{W}-\pi)
. Take
\mathsf{U}_{n}
to be the latent variable from Lemma
SA-1
. Then we decompose

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1}=\Delta_{2,2,1,a}+\Delta_{2,2,1,b}+\Delta_{2,2,1,c}+\Delta_{2,2,1,d},

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1,a}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}(\mathbf{W}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])^{\operatorname{T}}\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{W}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1,b}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi)^{\operatorname{T}}\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{W}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1,c}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}(\mathbf{W}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])^{\operatorname{T}}\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1,d}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi)^{\operatorname{T}}\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi).

Since
\lVert\mathbf{H}\rVert_{2}\leq\lVert\mathbf{H}\rVert_{F}\leq\frac{B}{2\pi}\sqrt{n}(\min_{i}N_{i})^{-1/2}
, we can apply Hanson-Wright inequality conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1,a}=O_{\psi_{1}}(n^{\frac{1}{2}-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}(\min_{i}N_{i})^{-1/2}).

Since
g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)
’s are independent to
W_{i}
, by Lemma
SA-3
,

n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(W_{i}-\pi)g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)=O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}(1).

By Equation
SA-8
, Lipschitzness of
\tanh
and Lemma
SA-2
,
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})
, hence

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1,b}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi\right)n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}-\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]}{\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)=O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}\left((\log n)^{-1/2}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}\right).

Then by concentration of
\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}
from Lemma
SA-27
, we have

\displaystyle|\Delta_{2,2,1,c}|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi}{2\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]}n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}\left|\frac{\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi}{2\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]}\right|\cdot\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2},tc}\left(\log n\max_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}\right)+O_{\psi_{\beta,\gamma},tc}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

The bound for
\Delta_{2,2,1,d}
follows from the definition of
\mathbf{H}
and
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1,d}=n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}\left(\tanh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h\right)\right]\right)^{2}=O_{\psi_{\beta,\gamma}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

For
\Delta_{2,2,2}
, a Taylor expansion of
p_{i}
in terms of
\mathscr{m}_{i}
, and the concentration of
\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}
in Lemma
SA-27
implies that

\displaystyle|\Delta_{2,2,2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|p_{i}^{-1}-\mathbb{E}[p_{i}]^{-1}|\cdot\Big|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|\exp(-2\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}-2h)-\mathbb{E}[\exp(-2\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}-2h)]|\cdot\Big|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}|\mathscr{m}-\pi|\cdot\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\Big|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\Big|+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2},tc}\left(\log n\max_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}\right)+O_{\psi_{2},tc}(n^{-1/2}).

SA-10.5
Proof of Lemma
SA-13

Throughout the proof, the Ising spins
\mathbf{W}=(W_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
are distributed according to Assumption
SA-1
with parameters
(\beta,h)
. For brevity, we write
\mathbb{P}
in place of
\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}
.

Take
\mathsf{U}_{n}
to be the latent variable given in Lemma
SA-1
. We further decompose by

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1}=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast})\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{i}-\pi)\right)^{2}=\Delta_{2,3,1,a}+\Delta_{2,3,1,b}+\Delta_{2,3,1,c},

where
\eta_{i}^{\ast}
is some value between
\pi
and
M_{i}/N_{i}
, and

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,a}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast})\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,b}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast})\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)\left(\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi\right),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,c}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast})\left(\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi\right)^{2}.

(SA-28)

Part I:
\Delta_{2,3,1,c}
.

Since
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{U}]=\tanh\left(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h\right)
, we have
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})
and
(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi)^{2}=O_{\psi_{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}/2}}(n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})
. It then follows from boundness of
g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast})
that

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,c}=O_{\psi_{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}/2}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

Part II:
\Delta_{2,3,1,b}
.

Condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
W_{i}
’s are i.i.d. By Mc-Diarmid inequality conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
for each
\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])
and using a union bound over
i\in[n]
, for all
i\in[n]
, for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\Delta_{2,3,1,b}\right|\geq 2\max_{i}N_{i}^{-1/2}n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}|\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi|\sqrt{t}\middle|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}\right)\leq 2n\exp(-t).

The tails for
n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi)
are also controlled,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}\left|\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi\right|\geq C_{\beta,h}(\log n)^{1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}\right)\leq n^{-1/2}.

Integrate over the distribution of
\mathsf{U}_{n}
and using a union bound, for large
n
, for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{2,3,1,b}|\geq 2C_{\beta,h}\max_{i}N_{i}^{-1/2}t^{1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}\middle|\mathbf{E}\right)\leq 2n\exp(-t)+C_{\beta,h}n^{-1/2}.

By Equation
SA-26
, condition on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,
\mathbb{P}(N_{i}\leq\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]/3|\mathbf{U})\leq n^{-100}
. Hence for such
\mathbf{U}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\Delta_{2,3,1,b}|\geq 4C_{\beta,h}\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}t^{1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}\middle|\mathbf{U}\right)\leq 2n\exp(-t)+C_{\beta,h}n^{-1/2}.

In other words, conditional on
\mathbf{U}
s.t.
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,b}=O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}(\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}).

Part III:
\Delta_{2,3,1,a}
.

For notational simplicity, we will denote

\displaystyle B_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast})\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\theta\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)^{2}=:F(\mathbf{W},\mathsf{U}_{n}),

and since we assume
g_{i}(\ell,\cdot)
is
C^{4}
for
\ell\in\{-1,1\}
, we know
\theta(\ell,\cdot)
is
C^{2}
for
\ell\in\{-1,1\}
. Then we can decompose
\Delta_{2,3,1,a}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,1,a}|\mathbf{E}]
as

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,a}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,1,a}|\mathbf{E}]

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(B_{i}-\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]\right)+n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]-\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathbf{E}]\right).

where
F
is a function that possibly depends on
\beta(\mathbf{U})
and
\mathbf{E}
.

First part of
\Delta_{2,3,1,a}
:
The first two terms have a quadratic form in
W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]
, except for the term
\theta(M_{i}/N_{i})
. We will handle it via a generalized version of Hanson-Wright inequality. Fix
\mathsf{U}_{n}
and
\mathbf{E}
, consider

\displaystyle H(\mathbf{W})=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\theta\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)^{2}.

Denoting by
D_{k}H
the partial derivative of
H
w.r.p to
W_{k}
and
D_{k,l}
the mixed partials, then

\displaystyle D_{k}H(\mathbf{W})=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\neq k}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\prime}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\frac{E_{ik}}{N_{i}}\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\neq k}^{n}\theta\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)2\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)\frac{E_{ik}}{N_{i}}.

Since we have assumed
f
is at least
4
-times continuously differentiable, we can apply standard concentration inequalities for
\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])
to get

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[D_{k}H(\mathbf{W})|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]|\lesssim n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ik}N_{i}^{-3/2}.

Hence the gradient of
H
is bounded by

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbb{E}[DH(\mathbf{W})|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]\rVert_{2}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}n^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ik}N_{i}^{-3/2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}n^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{ik}N_{i}^{-3}+\sum_{j_{1}=1}\sum_{j_{2}\neq j_{1}}\frac{E_{j_{1}k}E_{j_{2}k}}{N_{j_{1}}^{3/2}N_{j_{2}}^{3/2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}^{2}}{\min_{i}N_{i}^{3}}.

Moreover, the mix partials are

\displaystyle D_{k,l}H(\mathbf{W})=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\neq k,l}^{n}\theta^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)^{2}\frac{E_{ik}E_{il}}{N_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle+2n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta^{\prime}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)2\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)\frac{E_{ik}E_{il}}{N_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\frac{E_{ik}E_{il}}{N_{i}^{2}}.

Hence
\lVert D_{k,l}H(\mathbf{W})\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ik}E_{il}}{N_{i}^{2}}
. Hence

\displaystyle\lVert\lVert HF\rVert_{F}^{2}\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ik}E_{il}}{N_{i}^{2}}\right)^{2}\lesssim n^{-1}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\frac{E_{i_{1}l}}{N_{i_{1}}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{E_{i_{1}k}}{N_{i_{1}}}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\frac{E_{i_{2}k}}{N_{i_{2}}}\frac{1}{N_{i_{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}}{\min_{i}N_{i}^{2}}.

Moreover, since
HF
is symmetric,

\displaystyle\lVert\lVert HF\rVert_{2}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lVert\lVert HF\rVert_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim\max_{k}\sum_{l=1}^{n}n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ik}E_{il}}{N_{i}^{2}}\lesssim n^{-1/2}\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}}{\min_{i}N_{i}}.

Hence by Theorem 3 from
[
5
]
, for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(B_{i}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}])\bigg|\geq t\bigg|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}^{2}}{\min_{i}N_{i}^{3}}+\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}}{\min_{i}N_{i}^{2}}},\frac{t}{n^{-1/2}\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}}{\min_{i}N_{i}}}\right)\right).

By Equation
SA-26
and a similar argument for upper bound, for each
i\in[n]
, conditional on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
,
\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]/2\leq N_{i}\leq 2\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]
. Hence for each
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(B_{i}-\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}])\right|\geq 8\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}\sqrt{t}+8C_{\beta,h}n^{-1/2}t\middle|\mathbf{U}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\exp(-t)

\displaystyle\qquad+n^{-99},

that is

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(B_{i}-\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]\right)=O_{\psi_{2},tc}\left(n^{\frac{1}{2}-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\max\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}\right)+O_{\psi_{1},tc}\left(n^{-1/2}\right).

(SA-29)

Second part of
\Delta_{2,3,1,a}
:
Next, we will show
n^{1-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[B_{i}\middle|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{U},\mathbf{E}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[B_{i}|\mathbf{E}\right]\right)
, is small. There exists a function
F
that possibly depends on
\beta
and
\mathbf{E}
such that

\displaystyle F(\mathbf{W},\mathsf{U}_{n})=\frac{1}{2}\theta\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])\right)^{2}.

Define
p(u)=\mathbb{P}(W_{j}=1|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{U})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u,\mathbf{U},\mathbf{E}]=\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W},\mathsf{U}_{n})|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u]=\sum_{\mathbf{w}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}p(u)^{w_{l}}(1-p(u))^{1-w_{l}}F(\mathbf{w},u).

Using chain rule and product rule for derivatives,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\mathbb{E}\left[B_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u,\mathbf{U}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{w}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}\Bigg[\sum_{l=1}^{n}\prod_{s\neq l}p(u)^{w_{s}}(1-p(u))^{1-w_{s}}\left(F((\mathbf{w}_{-l},w_{l}=1),u)-F((\mathbf{w}_{-l},w_{l}=-1),u)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\prod_{i=1}^{n}p(u)^{w_{i}}(1-p(u))^{1-w_{i}}\partial_{u}F(\mathbf{w},u)\Bigg]p^{\prime}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{W}_{-l}}\left[F((\mathbf{W}_{-l},W_{l}=1),u)-F((\mathbf{W}_{-l},W_{l}=-1),u)\right]p^{\prime}(u)+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{W}}\left[\partial_{u}F(\mathbf{W},u)\right]p^{\prime}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{i}}}\frac{E_{il}}{N_{i}}\right)\lVert p^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}+O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{i}}}\lVert p^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}\right)\lVert p^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}=O_{\mathbb{P}}((nN_{i})^{-0.5}),

where in the last line, we have used

\displaystyle|D_{W_{l}}F(\mathbf{w},u)|\lesssim\lVert\theta^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}\frac{E_{il}}{N_{i}}\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|U,\mathbf{U}])\right)^{2}+\lVert\theta\rVert_{\infty}\left|\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|U,\mathbf{U}])\right|\frac{E_{il}}{N_{i}},

\displaystyle|\partial_{u}F(\mathbf{w},u)|\lesssim\lVert\theta\rVert_{\infty}\lVert p^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}\left|\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|U,\mathbf{U}])\right|,

and that fact that
\lVert p^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}=O((2\beta/n)^{0.5})
and Hoeffiding’s inequality for
\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{u}\mathbb{E}\left[F(\mathbf{w},\mathsf{U}_{n})\middle|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u,\mathbf{E}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\partial_{u}F(\mathbf{w},\mathsf{U}_{n})\right|\middle|\mathsf{U}_{n}=u\right]=O\left(n^{-1/2}\min_{i}N_{i}^{-1/2}\right).

(SA-30)

Since
\mathsf{U}_{n}=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}(n^{\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}-1/2})
, we have

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{U}]-\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathbf{U}]\right)

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}\left(n^{1-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}n^{-1/2}\min_{i}N_{i}^{-1/2}n^{\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}\left(\min_{i}N_{i}^{-1/2}\right).

(SA-31)

Combining Equations
SA-29
and
SA-31
, conditional on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(B_{i}-\mathbb{E}[B_{i}|\mathbf{E}]\right)

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2},tc}\left(n^{\frac{1}{2}-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\max\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}\right)+O_{\psi_{1},tc}\left(n^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}\left(\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}]^{-1/2}\right).

Combining the bounds for
\Delta_{2,3,1,a},\Delta_{2,3,1,b},\Delta_{2,3,1,c}
, we get the desired result.

SA-10.6
Proof of Lemma
SA-14

Throughout the proof, the Ising spins
\mathbf{W}=(W_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
are distributed according to Assumption
SA-1
with parameters
(\beta,h)
. For brevity, we write
\mathbb{P}
in place of
\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}
.

Recall

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,2}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\frac{W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathbf{W}_{-i}]}{p_{i}}\left[g_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)-g_{i}^{\prime}\left(1,\pi\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)\right].

First, we will consider the effect of fluctuation of
p_{i}
and
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathbf{W}_{-i}]
. Recall

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathbf{W}_{-i}]=\tanh\left(\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}+h\right),\quad p_{i}=\left(1+\exp\left(-2\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}-2h\right)\right)^{-1}.

It follows from the boundeness of
\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}+h
,
\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})
that for each
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\frac{W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathbf{W}_{-i}]}{p_{i}}=2\frac{W_{i}-\pi}{\pi+1}+O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

Moreover for some
\eta_{i}^{\ast}
between
M_{i}/N_{i}
and
\pi
, using Lemma
SA-27
we have

\displaystyle g_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-g_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)-g_{i}^{\prime}\left(1,\pi\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{i}^{\prime\prime}(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast})\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)^{2}=O_{\psi_{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}/2},tc}(n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})+O_{\psi_{1},tc}(N_{i}^{-1}).

Using a union bound over
i
and an argument for the product of two terms with bounded Orlicz norm with tail control, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,2}=

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{W_{i}-\pi}{\pi+1}\left[g_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-g_{i}(1,\pi)-g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)\right]

\displaystyle+O_{\psi_{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}/2},tc}((\log n)^{-1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})+O_{\psi_{1},tc}((\log n)^{-1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}N_{i}^{-1}).

Next, we will show
n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{W_{i}-\pi}{\pi+1}\left[g_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-g_{i}(1,\pi)-g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)\right]
is small. Suppose
g_{i}(1,\cdot)
is
p
-times continuously differentiable. Define

\displaystyle\delta_{p}=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{W_{i}-\pi}{\pi+1}g_{i}^{(p)}\left(1,\pi\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)^{p}.

We will use the conditioning strategy to analyse
\delta_{p}
: Decompse by

\displaystyle\delta_{p}=\delta_{p,1}+\delta_{p,2}+\delta_{p,3},

with

\displaystyle\delta_{p,1}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]}{\pi+1}g_{i}^{(p)}\left(1,\pi\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)^{p},

\displaystyle\delta_{p,2}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi}{\pi+1}g_{i}^{(p)}\left(1,\pi\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)^{p},

\displaystyle\delta_{p,3}

\displaystyle=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{W_{i}-\pi}{\pi+1}g_{i}^{(p)}(1,\pi)\left[\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)^{p}-\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)^{p}\right].

First, we will show
\delta_{p,2}
and
\delta_{p,3}
are small. By Hoeffding inequality,
M_{i}/N_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]=O_{\psi_{2}}(N_{i}^{-1/2})
. Moreover,
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi=O_{\psi_{\beta,h}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})
. Hence

\displaystyle\delta_{p,2}=O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}(\max_{i}N_{i}^{-1/2}).

For
\delta_{p,3}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)^{p}-\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=p\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\xi^{\ast}\right)^{p-1}\left(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi\right),

where
\xi^{\ast}
is some quantity between
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]
and
\pi
. Since
x\mapsto x^{p-1}
is either monotone or convex and none-negative, condition on
\mathbf{E}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\xi^{\ast}\right|^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right|^{p-1},\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|^{p-1}\right\}

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{\frac{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}{p-1}}}(n^{-(p-1)\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})+O_{\psi_{\frac{2}{p-1}}}(N_{i}^{-\frac{p-1}{2}}).

Combining with boundedness of
g_{i}^{(p)}(1,\pi)
and tail control of
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{p,3}

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{\frac{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}{p-1}}}\left((\log n)^{\frac{1}{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}}n^{-(p-1)\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}\right)+O_{\psi_{\frac{2}{p-1}}}\left((\log n)^{\frac{1}{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}}N_{i}^{-\frac{p-1}{2}}\right).

For
\delta_{p,1}
, we will again use the generalized version of Hanson-Wright inequality. For each
k\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\partial_{k}\delta_{p,1}=

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i\neq k}\frac{W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]}{\pi+1}g_{i}^{(p)}(1,\pi)p\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)^{p-1}\frac{E_{ik}}{N_{i}}

\displaystyle\qquad+n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}g_{k}^{(p)}(1,\pi)\left(\frac{M_{k}}{N_{k}}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)^{p}.

Hence condition on
\mathbf{E}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla\delta_{p,1}\right]\rVert=O\left(n^{1/2-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}N_{i}^{-(p-1)/2}\right).

Taking mixed partials w.r.p
\delta_{p,1}
and using boundedness of
g_{i}^{(p)}
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{k}\partial_{l}\delta_{p,1}\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i\neq k,l}\frac{E_{ik}E_{il}}{N_{i}^{2}}+n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\frac{E_{lk}}{N_{l}}+n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\frac{E_{kl}}{N_{k}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\lVert\lVert\operatorname{Hess}(\delta_{p,1})\rVert_{2}\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim\lVert\lVert\operatorname{Hess}(\delta_{p,1})\rVert_{F}\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim n^{1/2-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\left(\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}^{3}}{\min_{i}N_{i}^{4}}\right)^{1/2}.

It then follows from Equation
SA-26
and Theorem 3 in
[
5
]
that conditional on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,

\displaystyle\delta_{p,1}-\mathbb{E}[\delta_{p,1}|\mathbf{E}]=O_{\psi_{1},tc}\left(n^{1/2-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\left(\frac{\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{3}}{\min_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{4}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

Trade-off Between Smoothness of
g_{i}(1,\cdot)
and Sparsity of Graph

Assume
g_{i}(1,\cdot)
is
p+1
-times continuously differentiable. Then by the decomposition of
\Delta_{2,3,2}
, condition on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,2}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,2}|\mathbf{E}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=2}^{p}\delta_{l}-\mathbb{E}[\delta_{l}|\mathbf{E}]+n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\frac{Y_{i}^{(p+1)}(1,\xi_{i}^{\ast})}{(p+1)!}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)^{p+1}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{Y_{i}^{(p+1)}(1,\xi_{i}^{\ast})}{(p+1)!}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)^{p+1}\middle|\mathbf{E}\right]\right]

\displaystyle+O_{\psi_{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}/2},tc}((\log n)^{-1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})+O_{\psi_{1},tc}((\log n)^{-1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}(\min_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}])^{-1}).

Then by the concentration of
M_{i}/N_{i}-\pi
given in Lemma
SA-27
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,2}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,2}|\mathbf{E}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\psi_{\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}/2},tc}((\log n)^{-1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})+O_{\psi_{1},tc}((\log n)^{-1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}(\min_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}])^{-1})

\displaystyle+O_{\psi_{1},tc}\left(n^{1/2-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\left(\frac{\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{3}}{\min_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{4}}\right)^{1/2}\right)+O_{\psi_{2/(p+1)},tc}\left(n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}(\min_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-(p+1)/2})\right).

SA-10.7
Proof of Lemma
SA-15

For notational simplicity, denote
\widehat{\mathscr{p}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}
and
\mathscr{p}=\frac{1}{2}\tanh(\beta\pi+h)+\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\pi+\frac{1}{2}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\mathscr{p}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}}\frac{\mathscr{p}-\widehat{\mathscr{p}}}{\mathscr{p}}.

Taylor expand
x\mapsto\tanh(\beta x+h)
at
x=\pi
, we have

\displaystyle 2(\widehat{\mathscr{p}}-\mathscr{p})=

\displaystyle\mathscr{m}-\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}+h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi+\mathscr{m}-\pi-\tanh(\beta\pi+h)-\beta\sech^{2}(\beta\pi+h)(\mathscr{m}-\pi)+O((\mathscr{m}-\pi)^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\beta\pi+h))(\mathscr{m}-\pi)+O((\mathscr{m}-\pi)^{2}),

where
O(\cdot)
is up to a universal constant. Together with concentration of
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}Y_{i}
towards
p\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\mathscr{p}}=-\frac{1-\beta(1-\pi^{2})}{1+\pi}\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})]+O_{\psi_{1}}(n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

A Taylor expansion of
g_{i}
and concentration of
M_{i}/N_{i}
then implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})]=\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\pi)]+\mathbb{E}[g_{i}^{(1)}(1,\pi)(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi)]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\pi^{\ast})(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi)^{2}]=O(n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}).

The conclusion then follows.

SA-10.8
Proof of Lemma
SA-16

Throughout the proof, the Ising spins
\mathbf{W}=(W_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
are distributed according to Assumption
SA-1
with parameters
(\beta,h)
. For brevity, we write
\mathbb{P}
in place of
\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}
.

By Lemma
SA-11
to Lemma
SA-15
, we show

\displaystyle n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n})

(SA-32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(R_{i}-\mathbb{E}[R_{i}]+b_{i})(W_{i}-\pi)+\varepsilon,

(SA-33)

where
R_{i}=\frac{g_{i}(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})}{1+\pi}+\frac{g_{i}(-1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})}{1-\pi}
, and
b_{i}=\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}\left(1,\pi\right)
, and
\varepsilon
is such that condition on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}=\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}:\min_{i\in[n]}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij}\geq 32\log n\}
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon=O_{\psi_{1},tc}\left(\log n\max_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}\right)+O_{\psi_{1},tc}(\sqrt{\log n}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}})

\displaystyle+O_{\psi_{1},tc}\bigg(n^{1/2-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\bigg(\frac{\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{3}}{\min_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{4}}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg)+O_{\psi_{2/(p+1)},tc}\left(n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}(\min_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-(p+1)/2})\right).

(SA-34)

Following the strategy as in the proof of Theorem 4 in
[
11
]
, we will show
b_{i}
is close to
R_{i}
: First, decompose by

\displaystyle\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-R_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{n\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)+\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{n\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-R_{i}.

By Equation
SA-26
, condition on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,

|\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{n\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)|\leq Cn^{-1/2}

with probability at least
1-n^{-99}
. Moreover,
\frac{E_{ij}}{\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi),j\neq i
are i.i.d condition on
U_{i}
, hence
\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{n\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-R_{i}=O_{\psi_{2}}((n\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]^{-1/2})=O_{\psi_{2}}(\mathbb{E}[N_{j}|X]^{-1/2})
. It follows that conditional on
\mathbf{U}
such that
A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}
,

\displaystyle\max_{i}|\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-R_{i}|=O_{\psi_{2},tc}(\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}).

(SA-35)

Again using the conditional i.i.d decomposition, Hoeffiding inequality and
\mathsf{U}_{n}
’s concentration for the two terms respectively,

\displaystyle|n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-R_{i}](W_{i}-\pi)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-R_{i}](W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])|

\displaystyle+n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}|\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi|\max_{i}|\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-R_{i}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\psi_{2}}(n^{\frac{1}{2}-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2})+O_{\psi_{\beta,h},tc}((\log n)^{1/\mathtt{p}_{\beta,h}}\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2})=\varepsilon^{\prime}.

(SA-36)

Hence denote the term of stochastic linearization by
G_{n}
, i.e.

\displaystyle G_{n}=n^{-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(R_{i}-\mathbb{E}[R_{i}]+Q_{i})(W_{i}-\pi).

Since
R_{i}-\mathbb{E}[R_{i}]+Q_{i}
’s are i.i.d independent to
W_{i}
’s with bounded third moment, we know from Lemma
SA-3
that
G_{n}
can be approximated by either a Gaussian or non-Gaussian law, that is order
1
, this gives

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}|\mathbf{U})\leq t\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(G_{n}\leq t|\mathbf{U}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\min_{u>0}\mathbb{P}\left(G_{n}\leq t+u\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(G_{n}\leq t\right)+\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq u)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\min_{u>0}\mathbb{P}\left(G_{n}\leq t+u\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(G_{n}\leq t+u\right)+\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq u)+\mathbb{P}\left(t\leq G_{n}\leq t+u\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(n^{-1/2})+\min_{u>0}\exp(-(u/\mathtt{r})^{\mathtt{a}})+\mathtt{c}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O((\log n)^{\mathtt{a}}\mathtt{r}(\mathbf{U})),

where
O(\cdot)
does not depend on the value of
\mathbf{U}
and

\displaystyle\mathtt{r}(\mathbf{U})=

\displaystyle n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}+\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}+n^{1/2-\mathtt{a}_{\beta,h}}\left(\frac{\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{3}}{\min\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{4}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-(p+1)/2}.

To analyse the second term, recall
\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]=\rho_{n}\sum_{j\neq i}G(U_{i},U_{j})
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i}\left(\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]\right)^{-1/2}\mathbbm{1}(A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(n\rho_{n})^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}G(U_{i},U_{j})\right)^{-1/2}\mathbbm{1}(A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{\log n}(n\rho_{n})^{-1/2}),

the last line is because with probability at least
1-n^{-98}
,
E=\{\frac{1}{2}g(U_{i})\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}G(U_{i},U_{j})\leq 2g(U_{i}),\forall 1\leq i\leq n\}
happens, and by maximal inequality,
\max_{i}|g(U_{i})|^{-1/2}=O_{\psi_{2}}(\sqrt{\log n})
. And on
\{A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}\}\cap E
,
\max_{i}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}G(U_{i},U_{j}))^{-1/2}\leq(32\log n/n)^{-1/2}
, since we assume
G
is positive. By similar argument for the last two terms in
\mathtt{r}(\mathbf{U})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[r(\mathbf{U})\mathbbm{1}(A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A})\right]

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}+\sqrt{\log n}(n\rho_{n})^{-1/2}+\sqrt{\log n}n^{\mathtt{r}_{\beta,h}}(n\rho_{n})^{-(p+1)/2}.

Recall that
\mathscr{A}=\{A(\mathbf{U}):\min_{i}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij}(\mathbf{U})\geq 32\log n\}
. Since
\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij}(\mathbf{U})\sim\operatorname{Bin}(n-1,\mathbb{E}[G(X_{1},X_{2})])
, we know from Chernoff bound for Binomials and union bound over
i
that
\mathbb{P}(A(\mathbf{U})\notin\mathscr{A})\leq n^{-99}
. The conclusion then follows.

SA-10.9
Proof of Lemma
SA-17

Our proof for Lemma
SA-11
to Lemma
SA-15
relies on the following devices:

(1) Taylor expansion of
\tanh(\cdot)
in the inverse probability weighting for unbiased estimator, and taylor expansion of
Y_{i}(\ell,\cdot)
at
\mathbb{E}[T_{i}]
for
\ell\in\{0,1\}
.
Then the higher order terms are in terms of
\mathscr{m}-\pi
and
\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi
. In Lemma
SA-4
(taking
X_{i}\equiv 1
), we show

\displaystyle\lVert\mathscr{m}\rVert_{\psi_{1}}\leq\mathtt{K}n^{-1/4},

and in Lemma
SA-27
, we show

\displaystyle\lVert\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\rVert_{\psi_{1}}\leq\mathtt{K}n^{-1/4}+\mathtt{K}(n\rho_{n})^{-1/2},

where
\mathtt{K}
is some constant that does not depend on
\beta
. This shows for the higher order terms, we always have

\displaystyle\mathscr{m}^{2}=\mathscr{m}(1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)),\qquad(M_{i}/N_{i})^{2}=(M_{i}/N_{i})(1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)),

where the
o_{\mathbb{P}}(\cdot)
terms does not depend on
\beta
.

(2) Condition i.i.d decomposition based on the de-Finetti’s lemma (Lemma
SA-1
). Suppose
\mathsf{U}_{n}
is the latent variable from Lemma
SA-1
, we use decompositions based on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
: For Lemma
SA-12
to Lemma
SA-14
, we break down higher order terms in the form

\displaystyle F(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{E})-\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{E})|\mathbf{E}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{E})-\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{E})|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n}]+\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{E})|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{E})|\mathbf{E}].

For the first part
F(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{E})-\mathbb{E}[F(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{E})|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n}]
, we use the conditional i.i.d of
W_{i}
’s given
\mathsf{U}_{n}
. For the second part, we use concentration from Lemma
SA-2
that there exists a constant
\mathtt{K}
not depending on
\beta
or
n
, such that
\lVert\mathsf{U}_{n}\rVert_{\psi_{1}}\leq\mathtt{K}n^{1/4}
and the effective term
\lVert\tanh(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n})\rVert_{\psi_{1}}\leq\mathtt{K}n^{-1/4}
. In particular, the rate of concentration for conditional i.i.d Berry-Esseen and concentration of
\tanh(\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{n}}\mathsf{U}_{n})
does not depend on
\beta
.

By the same proof from Lemma
SA-11
to Lemma
SA-15
, we can show in
\widehat{\tau}_{n}-\tau_{n}
, the second and higher order terms in terms of
W_{i}-\pi
can always be dominated by the first order terms, with a rate that does not depend on
\beta
.

The conclusion then follows from the two devices and the same proof logic of Lemma
SA-11
to Lemma
SA-15
.

SA-11
Proofs: Section
SA-5

SA-11.1
Proof of Lemma
SA-18

Define
g(U_{j})=\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]
, for
i\neq j
. Reordering the terms,

\displaystyle\overline{\tau}^{a}=\frac{n-1}{n^{2}}\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}h_{j}(1,M_{j}/N_{j})-\frac{1-T_{j}}{1-1/2}h_{j}(-1,M_{j}/N_{j}).

Hence
\tau^{a}_{(i)}-\overline{\tau}^{a}
has the representation given by

\displaystyle\tau^{a}_{(i)}-\overline{\tau}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\frac{T_{i}}{1/2}h_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}\Big)+\frac{1}{n}\frac{1-T_{i}}{1-1/2}h_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{1-T_{j}}{1-1/2}h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}\Big)

(SA-37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\Big(\frac{T_{i}}{1/2}h_{i}(1,0)-1/2\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(1,0)]\Big)+\frac{1}{n}\Big(\frac{1-T_{i}}{1-1/2}h_{i}(-1,0)-(1-1/2)\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(-1,0)]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+O_{\psi_{2,tc}}(n^{-1}(n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}})

(SA-38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\Big(\frac{h_{i}(1,0)}{1/2}+\frac{h_{i}(-1,0)}{1-1/2}\Big)(T_{i}-1/2)+O_{\psi_{2,tc}}(n^{-1}(n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}})

(SA-39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\Big(\frac{f_{i}(1,0)}{1/2}+\frac{f_{i}(-1,0)}{1-1/2}\Big)(T_{i}-1/2)+O_{\psi_{2,tc}}(n^{-1}(n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

(SA-40)

where the second to last line is due to
-\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{1/2}1/2(h_{i}(1,0)-\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(1,0)])+\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{1-1/2}(1-1/2)(h_{i}(-1,0)-\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(-1,0)])=-\frac{2}{n}\varepsilon_{i}+\frac{2}{n}\varepsilon_{i}=0
.

Now we look at
b
-part. For representation purpose, we look at only the treatment part. The control part can be analysized by in the same way. Reordering the terms,

\displaystyle\overline{\tau}^{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\tau_{(i)}^{b}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}\bigg[h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\Big)-h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bigg[h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\Big)-h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}\Big)\bigg].

Hence
\tau_{(i)}^{b}-\overline{\tau}^{b}
has the representation given by

\displaystyle\tau_{(i)}^{b}-\overline{\tau}^{b}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}\bigg[h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\Big)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\Big)\bigg].

(SA-41)

The analysis follows from a Taylor expansion of
h_{j}(1,\cdot)
. For some
\xi_{j,i}^{\ast}
between
\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}
and
0
for each
j,i
,

\displaystyle h_{j}\Big(1,\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\Big)=

\displaystyle h_{j}(1,0)+\partial_{2}h(1,0)\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-0\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{2,2}h(1,0)\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-0\Big)^{2}

(SA-42)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\partial_{2,2,2}h(1,\xi_{j,i}^{\ast})\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-0\Big)^{3},

(SA-43)

where we have used
\partial_{2}h_{j}(1,\cdot)=\partial_{2}[h(1,\cdot)+\varepsilon_{j}]=\partial_{2}h(1,\cdot)
.

Part 1: Linear Terms

\displaystyle\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\neq i}\frac{E_{lj}}{N_{j}^{(i)}}W_{l}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\sum_{l\neq\iota}\frac{E_{lj}}{N_{j}^{(\iota)}}W_{l}

(SA-44)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{n}E_{lj}W_{l}\bigg(\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(i)}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(\iota)}}\bigg)-\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}^{(i)}}W_{i}.

By a decomposition argument,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(i)}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(\iota)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(i)}}-\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(\iota)}}+\frac{1}{(n-1)n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(\iota)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{E_{ji}-E_{j\iota}}{N_{j}^{(i)}N_{j}^{(\iota)}}+\frac{1}{(n-1)n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(\iota)}}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}(n\rho_{n})^{-1}\frac{E_{ij}-\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(n-1)n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(\iota)}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}E_{lj}W_{l}\bigg(\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(i)}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{1}{N_{j}^{(\iota)}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(n\rho_{n})^{-1}\frac{E_{ij}-\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}E_{lj}W_{l}+\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{n}E_{lj}W_{l}}{N_{j}^{(i)}}O_{\psi_{2,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{n}E_{lj}W_{l}}{nN_{j}^{(\iota)}}.

Condition on
U_{j}
,
(E_{lj}W_{l}:l\neq j)
are i.i.d mean-zero, hence Bernstein inequality gives
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}E_{lj}W_{l}=O_{\psi_{2}}(\sqrt{n^{-1}\rho_{n}})+O_{\psi_{1}}(n^{-1})
, which implies

\displaystyle(n\rho_{n})^{-1}\frac{E_{ij}-\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}E_{lj}W_{l}

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}})+O_{\psi_{1}}((n\rho_{n})^{-2}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{n}E_{lj}W_{l}}{nN_{j}^{(\iota)}}

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2}}(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\rho_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}})+O_{\psi_{1}}(n^{-2}).

Putting back into Equation (
SA-44
),

\displaystyle\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}^{(i)}}W_{i}+O_{\psi_{1}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}}).

Looking at contribution from the first order term in Taylor expanding
h_{j}(1,\cdot)
to
\tau_{(i)}^{b}-\overline{\tau}^{b}
in Equation (
SA-41
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}\bigg[\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h(1,0)W_{i}\frac{1}{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}^{(i)}}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-W_{i}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{E_{ij}}{n\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}-W_{i}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}^{(i)}}\frac{n\rho_{n}g(U_{j})-N_{j}}{n\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-W_{i}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{E_{ij}}{n\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}}).

Since
(E_{ij}T_{j}/g(U_{j}):j\in[n])
are independent condition on
U_{i}
, standard concentration inequality gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}\bigg[\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-W_{i}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{E_{ij}}{n\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-W_{i}\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\frac{E_{ij}}{n\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{W_{i}}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{E_{ij}}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\bigg]+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}}).

Since we assumed
\partial_{2}h(1,0)=\partial_{2}f(1,0)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\partial_{2}f_{j}(1,0)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
where

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h(1,0)\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}\bigg[\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{W_{i}}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{E_{ij}\partial_{2}f_{j}(1,0)}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\bigg]+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}).

Together with the leading term in Equation (
SA-41
), we have

\displaystyle n\sum_{i\in[n]}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\partial_{2}h_{j}(1,0)\frac{T_{j}}{1/2}\bigg[\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg]+\tau_{(i)}^{a}-\overline{\tau}^{a}\bigg)\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg(\frac{2}{n_{q}}\sum_{j\in\pazocal{I}_{q}}\partial_{2}h_{j}(1,0)\frac{T_{j}}{\theta_{q}}\bigg[\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg]+\tau_{(i)}^{a}-\overline{\tau}^{a}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in[n]}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{E_{ij}\partial_{2}f_{j}(1,0)}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\bigg]+\frac{f_{i}(1,0)}{1/2}(T_{i}-1/2)\bigg)\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{E_{ij}\partial_{2}f_{j}(1,0)}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\bigg]+\frac{f_{i}(1,0)}{1/2}(T_{i}-1/2)\bigg)+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n}^{3})^{-1})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{l}^{2}}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{E_{ij}\partial_{2}f_{j}(1,0)}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\bigg]+\frac{f_{i}(1,0)}{1/2}(T_{i}-1/2)\Big)\cdot

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|U_{i}\bigg]}\Big(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{E_{ij}\partial_{2}f_{j}(1,0)}{\rho_{n}g(U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\bigg]+\frac{f_{i}(1,0)}{1/2}(T_{i}-1/2)\Big)\bigg]+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n}^{3})^{-1})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{s}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\mathbf{S}_{\ell}^{\top}]\mathbf{e}_{q}+O_{\psi_{1,tc}}((n\rho_{n}^{3})^{-1})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Part 2: Higher Order Terms

For the second order terms, first notice that if
l\notin[n]
, then

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\bigg)^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\bigg(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\bigg(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}+\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg)\frac{M_{j}(E_{ij}-E_{\iota j})-(E_{ij}W_{i}-E_{\iota j}W_{\iota})N_{j}+E_{ij}E_{\iota j}(W_{i}-W_{\iota})}{N_{j}^{(i)}N_{j}^{(\iota)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\psi_{2,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}}),

where we have used
(M_{j}/N_{j})_{\iota}=O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}})
and
N_{j}^{-1}=O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-1})
. If
l\in[n]
, then again

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\bigg)^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\bigg(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\bigg)^{2}-\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\bigg(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{1}{(n-1)n}\sum_{\iota\in[n],\iota\neq l}\bigg(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\psi_{2,tc}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{3}{2}}).

Hence

\displaystyle n\sum_{i\in[n]}\bigg(\partial_{2,2}h(1,0)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{j\in[n]}T_{j}\bigg[\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\Big)^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\Big)^{2}\bigg]\bigg)\cdot

\displaystyle\bigg(\partial_{2,2}h(1,0)\frac{2}{n_{q}}\sum_{j\in\pazocal{I}_{q}}T_{j}\bigg[\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\Big)^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\Big)^{2}\bigg]\bigg)=O_{\psi_{2,tc}}((n\rho_{n}^{3})^{-1}).

For the third order residual, observe that
(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)})^{3}=O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-3/2})
. Then

\displaystyle n\sum_{i\in[n]}\bigg(\frac{2}{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[n]}T_{j}\bigg[\partial_{2,2,2}h\Big(1,\xi_{j,i}^{\ast}\Big)\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\Big)^{3}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\partial_{2,2,2}h\Big(1,\xi_{j,\iota}^{\ast}\Big)\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\Big)^{3}\bigg]\bigg)\cdot

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{2}{n_{q}}\sum_{j\in\pazocal{I}_{q}}T_{j}\bigg[\partial_{2,2,2}h\Big(1,\xi_{j,i}^{\ast}\Big)\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(i)}\Big)^{3}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\iota\in[n]}\partial_{2,2,2}h\Big(1,\xi_{j,\iota}^{\ast}\Big)\Big(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}_{(\iota)}\Big)^{3}\bigg]\bigg)

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2,tc}}((n\rho_{n}^{3})^{-1}).

The conclusion then follows from Equations (
SA-37
), (
SA-41
) and (
SA-42
).

SA-11.2
Proof of Lemma
SA-19

Define
\mathtt{r}(x)=(1,x)^{\top}
. Denote
\pi=\mathbb{E}[W_{i}]=2\mathbb{E}[T_{i}]-1
. Then

Case 1:
\beta<1

First, consider the gram-matrix.
Take
\zeta_{i}:=\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi)
. Then
1\lesssim\mathbb{V}[\zeta_{i}]\lesssim 1
. Take
b_{n}=\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}h_{n}
. Take

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}_{n}:=\frac{1}{nb_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)^{\top}K\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big),

where
\mathtt{r}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
is given by
\mathtt{r}(u)=(1,u)^{\top}
. Take
Q
to be the probability measure of
\zeta_{i}
given
\mathbf{E}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}:=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{B}_{n}|\mathbf{E}]=\begin{bmatrix}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{b_{n}}K(\frac{x}{b_{n}})dQ(x)&\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{x}{b_{n}}\frac{1}{b_{n}}K(\frac{x}{b_{n}})dQ(x)\\
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{x}{b_{n}}\frac{1}{b_{n}}K(\frac{x}{b_{n}})dQ(x)&\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\frac{x}{b_{n}})^{2}\frac{1}{b_{n}}K(\frac{x}{b_{n}})dQ(x)\end{bmatrix}.

In particular,
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{B})\gtrsim 1
. Now we want to show each entry of
\mathbf{B}_{n}
converge to those of
\mathbf{B}
. Take

\displaystyle F_{p,q}(\mathbf{W}):=\mathbf{e}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{e}_{q}=\frac{1}{nb_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)^{p+q}K\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big),\qquad p,q\in\{0,1\}.

Denote
\partial_{j}
to be the partial derivative w.r.p to
W_{j}
. Since
K
is Lipschitz with bounded support,

\displaystyle|\partial_{j}F_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\lesssim\frac{1}{b_{n}^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big|\partial_{j}\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\Big)\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{b_{n}^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}.

(SA-45)

Condition on
\mathbf{E}
,

\displaystyle F_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[F_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\mathbf{E}]+O_{\psi_{2}}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\partial_{j}F_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|^{2}\Big)=\mathbf{e}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{e}_{q}+O_{\psi_{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{nb_{n}^{4}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\Big)^{2}\Big).

Hence for all
p,q\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{e}_{q}=\mathbf{e}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{e}_{q}+O_{\psi_{2}}((nb_{n}^{4})^{-1}).

Since both
\mathbf{B}_{n}
and
\mathbf{B}
are two by two matrices,
\lVert\mathbf{B}_{n}-\mathbf{B}\rVert_{\operatorname{op}}\lesssim O_{\psi_{2}}((nb_{n}^{4})^{-1})
. By Weyl’s Theorem,

\displaystyle|\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{B}_{n})-\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{B})|\leq\lVert\mathbf{B}_{n}-\mathbf{B}\rVert_{\operatorname{op}}\lesssim(nb_{n}^{4})^{-1},

(SA-46)

and together with
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{B})\gtrsim 1
, implies
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{B}_{n})\gtrsim 1
. Take

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{n}:=\frac{1}{nb_{n}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)^{\top}K^{2}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)\mathbb{V}[Y_{i}|\zeta_{i}].

Hence variance can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}[\widehat{\gamma}_{0}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{W}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{B}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{0}\lesssim(nb_{n})^{-1},

(SA-47)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}[\widehat{\gamma}_{1}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{W}]

\displaystyle=n\rho_{n}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{B}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{1}\lesssim(n\rho_{n})(nb_{n}^{3})^{-1}=\rho_{n}b_{n}^{-3}.

(SA-48)

Next, consider the bias term. Since
f(1,\cdot)\in C^{2}
, whenever
|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi|\leq h_{n}=(n\rho_{n})^{-1/2}b_{n}
,

\displaystyle f(1,M_{i}/N_{i})

\displaystyle=f(1,\pi)+\partial_{2}f(1,\pi)\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\Big)+O\Big(\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\Big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=f(1,\pi)+\partial_{2}f(1,\pi)\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\Big)+O((n\rho_{n})^{-1}b_{n}^{2}).

Hence using the fourth and third lines above respectively,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}_{0}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{W}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{B}_{n}^{-1}\bigg[\frac{1}{nb_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)K\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)f\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)\bigg]

(SA-49)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{B}_{n}^{-1}\bigg[\frac{1}{nb_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)K\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)\bigg(\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)^{\top}(f(1,\pi),\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}\partial_{2}f(1,\pi))^{\top}+O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}})\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=f(1,\pi)+O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}_{1}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{W}]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{B}_{n}^{-1}\bigg[\frac{1}{nb_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)K\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)f\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{B}_{n}^{-1}\bigg[\frac{1}{nb_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)K\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)\bigg(\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i}}{b_{n}}\Big)^{\top}(f(1,\pi),\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}\partial_{2}f(1,\pi))^{\top}+O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-1})\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\partial_{2}f(1,\pi)+O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

Putting together Equations (
SA-47
) and (
SA-49
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}_{0}-\gamma_{0}=O_{\mathbb{P}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}+(nb_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\quad\widehat{\gamma}_{1}-\gamma_{1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\rho_{n}b_{n}^{-3}).

Hence any
b_{n}
such that
b_{n}=\Omega(n^{-1/4}+\rho_{n}^{1/3})
will make
(\widehat{\gamma}_{0},\widehat{\gamma}_{1})
a consistent estimator for
(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1})
. For any
0\leq\rho_{n}\leq 1
such that
n\rho_{n}\rightarrow\infty
, such a sequence
b_{n}
exists.

Case 2:
\beta=1

The order
\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}
is
n^{-1/4}
if
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}n\rho_{n}^{2}>c
for some
c>0
; and is
(n\rho_{n})^{-1/2}
if
n\rho_{n}^{2}=o(1)
. We consider these two cases separately.

Case 2.1:
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}n\rho_{n}^{2}>c
for some
c>0

Take
\eta_{i}=n^{\frac{1}{4}}(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi)
. Take
d_{n}=n^{1/4}h_{n}
. And with the same
\mathtt{r}
defined in Case 1,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{n}:=\frac{1}{nd_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\eta_{i}}{d_{n}}\Big)\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\eta_{i}}{d_{n}}\Big)^{\top}K\Big(\frac{\eta_{i}}{d_{n}}\Big),\qquad\mathbf{D}=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{D}_{n}].

Under the assumption
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}n\rho_{n}^{2}\leq c
for some
c>0
, we have
1\lesssim\mathbb{V}[\eta_{i}]\lesssim 1
. Hence
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{D})\gtrsim 1
. To study the convergence between
\mathbf{D}_{n}
and
\mathbf{D}
, again consider for
p,q\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W}):=\mathbf{e}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{n}\mathbf{e}_{q}=\frac{1}{nd_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{\eta_{i}}{d_{n}}\Big)^{p+q}K\Big(\frac{\eta_{i}}{d_{n}}\Big)=\frac{1}{n^{5/4}h_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(h_{n}^{-1}(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi)\Big)^{p+q}K\Big(h_{n}^{-1}(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi)\Big).

Still let
\mathsf{U}_{n}
be the latent variable from Lemma
SA-1
,
W_{i}
’s are independent conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
. Hence by similar argument as Equation (
SA-45
), we can show

\displaystyle G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})=\mathbb{E}[G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]+O_{\psi_{2}}((nd_{n}^{4})^{-1}).

Moreover, recall we denote by
\omega_{i}\in[k]
the block unit
i
belongs to, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{W}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}p(\mathsf{U}_{n,\omega_{i}})^{W_{s}}(1-p(\mathsf{U}_{n,\omega_{i}}))^{1-W_{s}}G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W}),

p(U_{l})=\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=1|U_{\ell})=\frac{1}{2}(\tanh(\sqrt{\beta_{\ell}/n}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h_{\ell})+1)
,
i\in\pazocal{I}_{\ell}
. Take the derivative term by term,

\displaystyle\partial_{U_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]=\sum_{j\in\pazocal{I}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{W}_{-j}}[G_{p,q}(W_{j}=1,W_{-j})-G_{p,q}(W_{j}=-1,W_{-j})]p^{\prime}(U_{\ell}).

Using Lipschitz property of
x\mapsto(x/h_{n})^{p+q}K(x/h_{n})
,

\displaystyle|G_{p,q}(W_{j}=1,W_{-j})-G_{p,q}(W_{j}=-1,W_{-j})|\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{5/4}h_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{h_{n}}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}.

Hence for all
\ell\in\mathscr{C}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{U_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]|\lesssim\sum_{j\in\pazocal{I}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{n^{5/4}h_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{h_{n}}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\lVert p^{\prime}\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{3/4}h_{n}^{2}}.

Moreover, for all
\ell\in\mathscr{C}
,
\lVert U_{\ell}\rVert_{\varphi_{2}}\lesssim n^{1/4}
. Together, this gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\mathsf{U}_{n},\mathbf{E}]-\mathbb{E}[G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\mathbf{E}]=O_{\mathbb{P}}((n^{1/2}h_{n}^{2})^{-1})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(d_{n}^{-2}).

Hence if we take
d_{n}\gg 1
(which implies
nd_{n}^{4}\gg 1
), then
G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})=\mathbb{E}[G_{p,q}(\mathbf{W})|\mathbf{E}]+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, implying
\lVert\mathbf{D}_{n}-\mathbf{D}\rVert_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{D}_{n})-\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{D})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, making
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{D}_{n})\gtrsim_{\mathbb{P}}1
. Take

\displaystyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{n}:=\frac{1}{nd_{n}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\eta_{i}}{d_{n}}\Big)\mathtt{r}\Big(\frac{\eta_{i}}{d_{n}}\Big)^{\top}K^{2}\Big(\frac{\eta_{i}}{d_{n}}\Big)\mathbb{V}[Y_{i}|\eta_{i}].

Hence variance can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}[\widehat{\gamma}_{0}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{W}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{D}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{0}\lesssim(nd_{n})^{-1},

(SA-50)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}[\widehat{\gamma}_{1}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{W}]

\displaystyle=n^{1/2}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{D}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{1}\lesssim n^{1/2}(nd_{n}^{3})^{-1}=n^{-1/2}d_{n}^{-3}.

(SA-51)

By similar argument as in Case 1, assume
d_{n}\gg 1
, we can show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}_{0}|\mathbf{E}]-\gamma_{0}=O(n^{-1/4}+n^{-1/2}d_{n}^{2}),\qquad\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma_{1}}|\mathbf{E}]-\gamma_{1}=O(n^{-1/4}d_{n}^{2}).

Hence if we choose
d_{n}
such that
1\ll d_{n}\ll n^{1/8}
, then

(\widehat{\gamma}_{0},\widehat{\gamma}_{1})
is a consistent estimator for
(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1})
. The only assumption we made for the existence of such a
d_{n}
is
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}n\rho_{n}^{2}\geq c
for some
c>0
.

Case 2.2:
n\rho_{n}^{2}=o(1)

Take
\eta_{i}:=\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi)
,
d_{n}=\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}h_{n}
. By similar decomposition based on latent variables, we can show if
n\rho_{n}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, then there exists
h_{n}
such that
(\widehat{\gamma}_{0},\widehat{\gamma}_{1})
is a consistent estimator for
(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1})
.

SA-12
Proofs: Section
SA-6

SA-12.1
Preliminary Lemmas

Lemma SA-28
.

Recall
\mathbf{W}=(W_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}
takes value in
\{-1,1\}^{n}
with

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{w}\right)=\frac{1}{Z}\exp\bigg(\frac{\beta}{n}\sum_{i<j}W_{i}W_{j}\bigg),\quad\beta>1.

Recall
\pi_{+}
and
\pi_{-}
are the positive and negative solutions to
x=\tanh(\beta x+h)
, respectively, and
\mathscr{m}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}
. Then
\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell]=\pi_{\ell}+O(n^{-1})
for
\ell=-,+
.

Proof.

The conditional concentration of
\mathscr{m}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}
towards
\pi_{\ell}
in Lemma
SA-3
implies,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|W_{-i},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell]|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}+h)\mathbbm{1}(\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}_{i})=\ell)|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell]+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(\beta\pi+h)+\sech^{2}(\beta\pi+h)(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi)-\sech^{2}(\beta m^{\ast}+h)\tanh(\beta m^{\ast}+h)(\mathscr{m}_{i}-\pi)^{2}]+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tanh(\beta\pi+h)+O(n^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi+O(n^{-1}),

where
\mathscr{m}^{\ast}
is a number between
\mathscr{m}
and
\pi
, and we have used boundedness of
\sech
.
∎

Lemma SA-29
.

Suppose Assumption
SA-1
, and Assumption 2, 3 hold with
h=0
and
\beta>1
. Then for
\ell=_{,}+
: (1) Condition on
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\right|=O_{\psi_{1}}(n^{-1/2})+O_{\psi_{2}}(N_{i}^{-1/2}).

(2) Define
A(\mathbf{U})=(G(U_{i},U_{j}))_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
. Condition on
\mathbf{U}
such that

A(\mathbf{U})\in\mathscr{A}=\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}:\min_{i\in[n]}\sum_{j\neq i}A_{ij}\geq 32\log n\}

for large enough
n
, for each
i\in[n]
and
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}\left(\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\right|\geq 4\mathbb{E}[N_{i}|\mathbf{U}]^{-1/2}t^{1/2}+Cn^{-1/2}t^{1/2}\middle|\mathbf{U},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell\right)\leq 2\exp(-t)+n^{-98},

where
C
is some absolute constant.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, the Ising spins
\mathbf{W}=(W_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
are distributed according to Assumption
SA-1
with parameters
(\beta,h)
. For brevity, we write
\mathbb{P}
in place of
\mathbb{P}_{\beta,h}
.

Let
\mathsf{U}_{n}
to be the latent variable defined in Lemma
SA-2
. Decompose by

\displaystyle\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}=\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\left(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)+\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi_{\ell}.

Condition on
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
W_{i}
’s are i.i.d. Berry-Esseen theorem gives that with
\mathsf{Z}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,1)
independent to
\mathsf{U}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\leq t\Big|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{v(\mathsf{U}_{n})}{N_{i}}}\mathsf{Z}+e(\mathsf{U}_{n})\leq t\Big|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n}\Big)\bigg|=O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

(SA-52)

where
e(\mathsf{U}_{n})=\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi=\tanh(\sqrt{\beta/n}\mathsf{U}_{n}+h)-\pi
, and
v(\mathsf{U}_{n})=\mathbb{V}[W_{i}-\pi|\mathsf{U}_{n}]
. By McDiarmid’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(|\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\left(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]\right)|\geq 2N_{i}^{-1/2}t\bigg|\mathbf{E}\bigg)\leq 2\exp(-t^{2}).
