Conclusion (1) then follows from the conditional concentration of
\mathsf{U}_{n}
in Remark
SA-1
.

Notice that
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathsf{U}_{n}
are independent to the random graph. Conclusion (1) and the same analysis as in Lemma
SA-27
give conclusion (2).
∎

SA-12.2
Proof of Lemma
SA-22

The result is a special case of Lemma
SA-16
in Section
SA-4
when
h\neq 0
.

SA-12.3
Proof of Theorem
SA-23

The result follows from Lemma
SA-3
, Lemma
SA-22
, and the same anti-concentration argument as in the proof of Lemma
SA-3
.

SA-12.4
Proof of Lemma
SA-20

I. The Unbiased Estimator

First, we consider the unbiased estimator

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{n,\text{UB}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{p_{i}}-\frac{(1-T_{i})Y_{i}}{1-p_{i}}\bigg],

with
p_{i}=\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=1|\mathbf{W}_{-i})=\left(\exp\left(-2\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}\right)+1\right)^{-1}
. Our analysis will be similar to the proofs in Section
SA-4.1
, but using the concentration of
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}
conditional on
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})
shown in Lemma
SA-3
instead of the unconditional concentration of
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}
. We decompose by

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}}{p_{i}}\bigg[g_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)-g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})\bigg].

For the first term, we Taylor expand the expression for
p_{i}^{-1}
in terms of
\mathscr{m}_{i}
, and get

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Y_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})+(c_{i,l}/2+d_{l})(W_{i}-\pi_{\ell}))+O_{\psi_{2},tc}(\sqrt{\log n}n^{-1/2}),

condition on
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell
, where

\displaystyle c_{i,l}=g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})(1+\exp(2\beta\pi_{\ell})),\qquad d_{l}=\frac{\beta(1+\exp(2\beta\pi_{\ell}))}{1+\cosh(2\beta\pi_{\ell})}\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})].

For the second term, we Taylor expand
g_{i}(1,\cdot)
at
\pi_{\ell}
: For some
\eta_{i}^{\ast}
between
\pi_{\ell}
and
\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}}{p_{i}}\bigg[g_{i}\bigg(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\bigg)-g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})\bigg]=\Delta_{2,1}^{\prime}+\Delta_{2,2}^{\prime}+\Delta_{2,3}^{\prime},

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,1}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{\prime}\left(1,\pi_{\ell}\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\right),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}^{\prime}\left(1,\pi_{\ell}\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\right),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}g_{i}^{\prime\prime}\left(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast}\right)}{2p_{i}}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\right)^{2}.

Term
\Delta_{2,1}^{\prime}
:
Denote
\mathbf{g}=(g_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}
. Rearranging the terms,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,1}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,1}^{\prime}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})\bigg](W_{i}-\pi_{\ell}).

Term
\Delta_{2,2}^{\prime}
:
Take
u_{\ell}=(\pi_{\ell}+1)/2
for
\ell\in\{-,+\}
. Decompose by

\Delta_{2,2}^{\prime}=\Delta_{2,2,1}^{\prime}+\Delta_{2,2,2}^{\prime},

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}-u_{\ell}}{u_{\ell}}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\Big),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}(p_{i}^{-1}-u_{\ell}^{-1})g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\Big).

Since
\beta<\infty
and
v_{-}
,
u_{-}
and
u_{+}
are bounded away from
0
and
1
. Rearranging the terms, we get

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2,1}^{\prime}=n^{-1}(\mathbf{W}-\pi_{\ell}\mathbf{1})^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\mathbf{W}-\pi_{\ell}\mathbf{1})

where
\mathbf{H}^{\ell}
is the
n\times n
matrix with
H^{\ell}_{ij}=g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})E_{ij}(2u_{\ell}N_{i})^{-1}
and
\mathbf{1}
is the
n
-dimensional vector with all entries
1
. To analyze the quadratic form, we use the same strategy as in the proof of Lemma
SA-12
: Let
\mathsf{U}_{n}
be the one defined in Lemma
SA-2
, and we know
W_{1},\cdots,W_{n}
are conditional i.i.d given
\mathsf{U}_{n}
. Then we can decompose
\Delta_{2,2,1}^{\prime}
into four terms based on

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}-\pi_{\ell}\mathbf{1}=(\mathbf{W}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}])+(\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{W}|\mathsf{U}_{n}]-\pi_{\ell}\mathbf{1}).

Conditional Berry-Esseen given
\mathsf{U}_{n}
, conditional concentration of
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
\mathscr{m}
and
\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}
given
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})
in Remark
SA-1
, Lemma
SA-3
and Lemma
SA-29
, and the same argument as in the proof for Lemma
SA-12
implies that condition on
\mathbf{g},\mathbf{E}
and
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})
,

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta_{2,2,j}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,2,j}^{\prime}|\mathbf{g},\mathbf{E},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})]\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\log(n)n^{-1/4}(\min_{i}N_{i})^{-1/2}+n^{-1/2},\qquad j=1,2.

Term
\Delta_{2,3}^{\prime}
:
Now we proceed to
\Delta_{2,3}^{\prime}
. Decompose by
\Delta_{2,3}^{\prime}=\Delta_{2,3,1}^{\prime}+\Delta_{2,3,2}^{\prime}
, where

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[g_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)-g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})-g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\Big)\Big],

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}\Big[g_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)-g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})-g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\Big)\Big].

Define
\Delta_{2,3,1,l}^{\prime}
to be the counterparts of
\Delta_{2,3,1,l}
in Equation
SA-28
with
\pi
by replaced by
\pi_{\ell}
for
l\in\{a,b,c\}
, the same argument in the proof of Lemma
SA-13
shows

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta_{2,3,1,a}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,1,a}^{\prime}|\mathbf{g},\mathbf{E},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell]\rVert_{\psi_{2},tc}=O((\min_{i}N_{i})^{-1/2}n^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta_{2,3,1,b}^{\prime}\rVert_{\psi_{2},tc}=O((\min_{i}N_{i})^{-1/2}n^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta_{2,3,1,c}^{\prime}\rVert_{\psi_{2},tc}=O(n^{-1}).

condition on
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell
for
\ell=-,+
. Combining the three parts,

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta_{2,3,1}^{\prime}\rVert_{\psi_{2},tc}=O((\min_{i}N_{i})^{-1/2}n^{-1/2}),

condition on
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell
for
\ell=-,+
. Taylor expanding
p_{i}=(1+\exp(-2\beta\mathscr{m}_{i}))^{-1}
as a function of
\mathscr{m}_{i}
at
\pi_{\ell}
, the same argument as in Lemma
SA-14
shows

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,2}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{W_{i}-\pi_{\ell}}{\pi_{\ell}+1}\Big[g_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)-g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})-g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\Big)\Big]+O_{\psi_{2},tc}((\min_{i}N_{i})^{-1/2}n^{-1/2}).

Conditional concentration of
\mathsf{U}_{n}
,
\mathscr{m}
and
\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}
given
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})
in Remark
SA-1
, Lemma
SA-3
and Lemma
SA-29
, and the same argument as in the proof for Lemma
SA-14
implies that condition on
\mathbf{g},\mathbf{E}
and
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})
,

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta_{2,3,2}^{\prime}\rVert_{\psi_{2},tc}=O((\min_{i}N_{i})^{-1/2}n^{-1/2}+(\min_{i}N_{i})^{-(p+1)/2})

Putting together.
Putting together the decompositions, condition on
\mathbf{E}
and
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{n,\text{UB}}-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{n,\text{UB}}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}L_{n,i,\ell}(W_{i}-\pi_{\ell})=O_{\psi_{2},tc}((\min_{i}N_{i})^{-\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}+(\min_{i}N_{i})^{-\frac{p+1}{2}}),

where with
c_{i,l}=g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})(1+\exp(2\beta\pi_{\ell}))
, and
d_{l}=\frac{\beta(1+\exp(2\beta\pi_{\ell}))}{1+\cosh(2\beta\pi_{\ell})}\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})]
,

\displaystyle L_{n,i,\ell}

\displaystyle=Y_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})+\Big(c_{i,l}/2+d_{l}+\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\pi_{\ell})\Big)(W_{i}-\pi_{\ell}).

Consider the event
\Omega_{i}=\{\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell,|\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}|\leq 1\}
and
\Omega=\cup_{1\leq i\leq n}\Omega_{i}
. We then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{\bigg(}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}=1{\bigg)}+\mathbb{P}{\bigg(}\sum_{j\neq i}W_{j}=-1{\bigg)}\leq C\exp(-nC).

implying
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{i})\leq C\exp(-nC)
,
1\leq i\leq n
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{n,\text{UB}}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}(1,M_{i}/N_{i})}{\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=1|W_{-i})}-\frac{(1-T_{i})Y_{i}(-1,M_{i}/N_{i})}{\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=-1|W_{-i})}\bigg)\mathbbm{1}(\Omega_{i}^{c})\bigg|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})\bigg]+O(\mathbb{P}(\Omega))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}(1,M_{i}/N_{i})}{\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=1|W_{-i},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}))}-\frac{(1-T_{i})Y_{i}(-1,M_{i}/N_{i})}{\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=-1|W_{-i},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}))}\bigg)\mathbbm{1}(\Omega_{i}^{c})\bigg|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})\bigg]+O(\mathbb{P}(\Omega))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}(1,M_{i}/N_{i})}{\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=1|W_{-i},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}))}-\frac{(1-T_{i})Y_{i}(-1,M_{i}/N_{i})}{\mathbb{P}(W_{i}=-1|W_{-i},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}))}\bigg|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})\bigg]+O(\mathbb{P}(\Omega))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{l}+O(C\exp(-Cn)).

(SA-53)

Hence condition on
\mathbf{E}
and
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{n,\text{UB}}-\tau_{n,\ell}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}L_{n,i,\ell}(W_{i}-\pi_{\ell})+O_{\psi_{2},tc}((\min_{i}N_{i})^{-\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}+(\min_{i}N_{i})^{-(p+1)/2}),

II. The Hajek Estimator

Now, we consider the difference between the unbiased estimator and the Hajek estimator. For notational simplicity, denote
\widehat{\mathscr{p}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}
and
\mathscr{p}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\tanh(\beta\pi_{\ell}+h)+\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\pi_{\ell}+\frac{1}{2}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\mathscr{p}_{\ell}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}}\frac{\mathscr{p}_{\ell}-\widehat{\mathscr{p}}}{\mathscr{p}_{\ell}}.

Taylor expand
x\mapsto\tanh(\beta x+h)
at
x=\pi_{\ell}
, we have

\displaystyle 2(\widehat{\mathscr{p}}-\mathscr{p}_{\ell})=

\displaystyle\mathscr{m}-\tanh(\beta\mathscr{m}+h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ell}+\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell}-\tanh(\beta\pi_{\ell}+h)-\beta\sech^{2}(\beta\pi_{\ell}+h)(\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})+O((\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\beta\pi_{\ell}+h))(\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})+O((\mathscr{m}-\pi_{\ell})^{2}),

where
O(\cdot)
is up to a universal constant. Together with the fact that condition on
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell
,
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{i}Y_{i}
concentrates towards
\mathscr{m}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\mathscr{p}_{\ell}}=-\frac{1-\beta(1-\pi_{\ell}^{2})}{1+\pi_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}\Big[g_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)\Big|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell\Big]+O_{\psi_{1}}(n^{-1}),

condition on
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell
. A Taylor expansion of
g_{i}
and concentration of
M_{i}/N_{i}
then implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[g_{i}\Big(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\Big)\Big|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\pi_{\ell})]+\mathbb{E}\Big[g_{i}^{(1)}(1,\pi_{\ell})\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\Big)\Big|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell\Big]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\pi^{\ast})\Big(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi_{\ell}\Big)^{2}\Big|\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m})=\ell\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(n^{-1}),

where
\pi^{\ast}
is some number between
\pi_{\ell}
and
M_{i}/N_{i}
. The conclusion then follows.

SA-12.5
Proof of Lemma
SA-21

The result follows from Lemma
SA-3
(3), Lemma
SA-20
, and the same anti-concentration argument as in the proof of Lemma
SA-3
.

SA-12.6
Proof of Lemma
SA-24

As in the case of one block analyzed in Section
SA-4
,
\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n}
is not an unbiased estimate of
\bm{\tau}_{n}
. We first consider an unbiased estimator to
\bm{\tau}_{n}
and then consider the difference.

I. The Unbiased Estimator

Consider
\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n,UB}=(\widehat{\tau}_{n,UB,1},\cdots,\widehat{\tau}_{n,UB,K})
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{n,UB,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}Y_{i}}{p_{i}}-\frac{(1-D_{i})Y_{i}}{1-p_{i}},\quad k\in[K].

Here
p_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbbm{1}(i\in\mathscr{C}_{k})(1+\exp(2\beta_{k}\mathscr{m}_{i,k}+2h_{k}))^{-1}
and
\mathscr{m}_{i,k}=n_{k}^{-1}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{k},j\neq i}W_{j}
.

Denote
\mathscr{m}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}
,
\mathscr{m}_{k}=n_{k}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}W_{i}
. For notational simplicity, we denote
\pi_{l,\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}_{l})}
by
\pi_{l}
for low temperature blocks
l\in\mathscr{L}
, and omit the index by
(\mathbf{s})
with
\mathbf{s}=\bm{sgn}
. As in the one-block case, we decompose by

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{n,UB,k}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{1,k}+\Delta_{2,k},

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}}{p_{i}}f_{i}\left(1,\zeta_{i}\right),\quad\Delta_{2,k}=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}}{p_{i}}\bigg(f_{i}\bigg(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\bigg)-f_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})\bigg),

and
\zeta_{i}=\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{K}N_{i,k}\pi_{k}}{\sum_{k=1}^{K}N_{i,k}}
.

I.1: Term
\Delta_{1,k}

Condition on
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g}=\{g_{i}:i\in[n]\}
and
\bm{sgn}
, the randomness of
\Delta_{1,k}
only comes from
(W_{i})_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}
, that is, the Ising bits from the same block. Hence

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,k}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{1,k}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\bm{sgn}]=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}Y_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}Y_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})\bigg|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\bm{sgn}\bigg].

The analysis in Lemma
SA-11
and Lemma
SA-20
with
g_{i}(1,\zeta_{k})\mathbbm{1}(i\in\mathscr{C}_{k})
replacing
g_{i}(1,\pi)
implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}Y_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{n_{k}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{D_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}Y_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})\mathbbm{1}(i\in\mathscr{C}_{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\left(\mathtt{c}_{k}Y_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})+\mathtt{d}_{k}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})]\right)(W_{i}-\pi_{k})+O_{\psi_{\beta_{k},h_{k}},tc}(n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}).

condition on
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\bm{sgn}
, where
\mathtt{c}_{k}=(1+\exp(2\beta_{k}\pi_{k}+2h_{k}))/2
and
\mathtt{d}_{k}=\beta_{k}(1+\exp(2\beta_{k}\pi_{k}+2h_{k}))/(1+\cosh(2\beta_{k}\pi_{k}+2h_{k}))
.

I.2: Term
\Delta_{2,k}

The linearization of
\Delta_{2,k}
involves
M_{i}/N_{i}
, which depends all blocks even if the estimator is for block
k
. We will find its stochastic linearization in terms of units in all blocks.

By a Taylor expansion of
g_{i}(1,\cdot)
at
\zeta_{i}

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}}{p_{i}}\bigg[g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\bigg(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\bigg)+g_{i}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\bigg(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\Delta_{2,1}+\Delta_{2,2}+\Delta_{2,3},

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\right),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\right),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\right)^{2},

with
g_{i}(x)=\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)Y_{i}^{(2)}(1,\zeta_{i}+t(x-\zeta_{i}))dt
. In particular,
g_{i}
is
C^{2}
.

Term
\Delta_{2,1}
:

Rearranging
\Delta_{2,1}
, we get the effective term in the stochastic linearization.

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\bigg[\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\pi_{l})\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{K}\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}\bigg[\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{k},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}Y_{j}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{j})\bigg](W_{i}-\pi_{l}).

Term
\Delta_{2,2}
:

We want to show
\Delta_{2,2}
is negligible. Consider the effect from each block separately. We claim that condition on
\mathbf{g}
,
\mathbf{E}
,
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\pi_{l})\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{K}\frac{1}{2n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{W_{i}-\pi_{k}}{(\pi_{k}+1)/2}g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\bigg(\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\pi_{l})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+O_{\psi_{2},tc}{\bigg(}\sqrt{\log n}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}{\bigg(}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}N_{i}^{-1/2}+\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}{\bigg)}{\bigg)}.

To get the second line, notice that
p_{i}=(1+\exp(2\beta_{k}\mathscr{m}_{i,k}+2h_{k}))^{-1}
is Lipschitz in
\mathscr{m}_{i,k}
, and since
(W_{i}:i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}),1\leq k\leq K
form independent Ising models, we can use Lemma
SA-3
to get
\mathscr{m}_{i,k}-\pi_{k}=O_{\psi_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}(n_{k}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}})
for
k\in\mathscr{H}\cup\mathscr{C}
, and condition on
\operatorname{sgn}(\mathscr{m}_{k})
,
\mathscr{m}_{i,k}-\pi_{k}=O_{\psi_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}(n_{k}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}})
for
k\in\mathscr{L}
. Hence for each
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{D_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}-\frac{W_{i}-\pi_{k}}{(\pi_{k}+1)/2}\right|

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}),\quad\text{condition on }\bm{sgn}.

Suppose
\mathsf{U}_{n,l}
is the latent variable underlining the distribution of
(W_{i}:i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}),l\in[K]
as in Lemma
SA-2
. Conditional on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
, using Hoeffiding’s inequality and the concentration of
\mathsf{U}_{n,l}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\pi_{l})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}])+\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}]-\pi_{l})

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2}}(N_{i}^{-1/2})+O_{\psi_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}).

From the fact that
\mathbb{P}(|Z_{1}Z_{2}|\geq t)\leq\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{\log n}|Z_{2}|\geq t)+\mathbb{P}(|Z_{1}|\geq\sqrt{\log n})
for any two random variables
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
, and using a union bound over the summation over
i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}
, we get the second line for
\Delta_{2,2}
.

Now consider the first term of
\Delta_{2,2}
. With the help of the latent variables
\mathsf{U}_{n,k},1\leq k\leq K
, decompose by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l}=\Gamma_{k,l,a}+\Gamma_{k,l,b}+\Gamma_{k,l,c}+\Gamma_{k,l,d},

where

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l,a}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}(W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,k}])(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}])\frac{g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})}{\pi_{k}+1}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\mathbbm{1}(i\neq j),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l,b}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}(W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,k}])g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\frac{N_{i,l}}{N_{i}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}]-\pi_{l}),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l,c}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,k}]-\pi_{k})g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\bigg(\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\pi_{l})\bigg)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l,d}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,k}]-\pi_{k})g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\frac{N_{i,l}}{N_{i}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}]-\pi_{l}).

Since conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n,k}
and
\mathsf{U}_{n,l}
,
(W_{i}:i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}\cup\mathscr{C}_{l})
are i.i.d., we can use Hoeffding’s inequality and boundedness of
g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})
to get conditional on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}(W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,k}])g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})\frac{N_{i,l}}{N_{i}}=O_{\psi_{2}}(n_{k}^{-1/2}),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\pi_{l})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}])+\frac{N_{i,l}}{N_{i}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}]-\pi_{l})

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2}}(N_{i}^{-1/2})+O_{\psi_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}(n_{l}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}).

It follows that condition on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l,b}-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{k,l,b}|\mathbf{E},\bm{sgn}]

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{1},tc}(\sqrt{\log n}n_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}n_{l}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l,c}-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{k,l,c}|\mathbf{E},\bm{sgn}]

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2},tc}(\sqrt{\log n}n_{k}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}N_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}})+O_{\psi_{1},tc}(\sqrt{\log n}n_{k}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}n_{l}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l,d}-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{k,l,d}|\mathbf{E},\bm{sgn}]

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{1},tc}(\sqrt{\log n}n_{k}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}n_{l}^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}).

For
\Gamma_{k,l,a}
, observe that with
\omega_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}k\mathbbm{1}(i\in\mathscr{C}_{k})
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k,l,a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}\sqcup\mathscr{C}_{l}}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{k}\sqcup\mathscr{C}_{l}}(W_{i}-\mathbb{E}[W_{i}|\mathsf{U}_{n,\omega_{i}}])(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,\omega_{j}}])H_{ij},

\displaystyle H_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{i}^{\prime}(1,\zeta_{i})}{v_{k}+1}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}\mathbbm{1}(i\in\mathscr{C}_{k},j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},i\neq j).

Apply Hanson-Wright inequality conditional on
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathsf{U}_{n,l}
and
\mathsf{U}_{n,k}
, we get

\Gamma_{k,l,a}-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{k,l,a}|\mathbf{E},\bm{sgn}]=O_{\psi_{1}}((n_{k}\min_{i}N_{i})^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Put together, conditional on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,2}|\mathbf{E},\bm{sgn}]=O_{\psi_{1},tc}\bigg(\sqrt{\log n}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}\bigg(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}N_{i}^{-1/2}+\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}\bigg)\bigg).

Term
\Delta_{2,3}
:

Similar to the analysis in Section
SA-4
, we decompose
\Delta_{2,3}=\Delta_{2,3,1}+\Delta_{2,3,2}
where

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1}=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}g_{i}\bigg(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\bigg)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\right)^{2},\quad\Delta_{2,3,2}=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}-p_{i}}{p_{i}}g_{i}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\right)^{2},

where
\Delta_{2,3,1}
is further decomposed based on latent variables
\mathsf{U}_{n,l},1\leq l\leq K
, that is,

\Delta_{2,3,1}=\Delta_{2,3,1,a}+\Delta_{2,3,1,b}+\Delta_{2,3,1,c},

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}g_{i}\bigg(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}])\bigg)^{2},

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,b}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}g_{i}\bigg(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}])\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{K}\frac{N_{i,l}}{N_{i}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}]-\pi_{l})\bigg),

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,c}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}g_{i}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{K}\frac{N_{i,l}}{N_{i}}(\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}]-\pi_{l})\bigg)^{2}.

Term
\Delta_{2,3,1,a}
:
Consider the
\Delta_{2,3,1,a}
as a (random) function on
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathsf{U}_{n,1},\cdots,\mathsf{U}_{n,K}
. Let

\displaystyle F_{i}(\mathbf{W},\mathsf{U}_{n,1},\cdots,\mathsf{U}_{n,k})=g_{i}\bigg(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}])\bigg)^{2}

Notice that conditional on
\mathsf{U}_{n,l},1\leq l\leq K
,
W_{j}
’s are independent random variables, and we can rewrite

\Delta_{2,3,1,a}=\frac{n}{n_{k}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\mathbbm{1}(i\in\mathscr{C}_{k})(\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}]))^{2}.

It follows from the same concentration argument for
\Delta_{2,3,1,a}
in the proof for Lemma
SA-13
that conditional on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,a}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,1,a}|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n,1},\cdots,\mathsf{U}_{n,K}]

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2},tc}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\max_{i}N_{i}^{-1/2})+O_{\psi_{1},tc}(n^{-1}).

Define
p_{l}(u)=\mathbb{E}[W_{i}=1|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}=u,i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}]
. Then we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F_{i}(\mathbf{W},\mathsf{U}_{n,1},\cdots,\mathsf{U}_{n,k})|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n,1}=u_{1},\cdots,\mathsf{U}_{n,k}=u_{k}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{w}\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}\prod_{l=1}^{K}\prod_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}p_{l}(u_{l})^{w_{i}}(1-p_{l}(u_{l}))^{1-w_{i}}F_{i}(\mathbf{W},u_{1},\cdots,u_{k}).

By the same argument as in the proof for Lemma
SA-14
,

\displaystyle\partial_{u_{l}}\mathbb{E}[F_{i}(\mathbf{W},\mathsf{U}_{n,1},\cdots,\mathsf{U}_{n,k})|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n,1}=u_{1},\cdots,\mathsf{U}_{n,k}=u_{k}]=O((nN_{i})^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

It then follows from the concentration of
\mathsf{U}_{n,1}
to
\mathsf{U}_{n,K}
that condition on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,1,a}|\mathbf{E},\mathsf{U}_{n,1},\cdots,\mathsf{U}_{n,K}]-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,1,a}|\mathbf{E}]

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2}}((nN_{i})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}).

Moreover, since
\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{l},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(W_{j}-\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}])=O_{\psi_{2}}(N_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}})
and
\mathbb{E}[W_{j}|\mathsf{U}_{n,l}]-\pi_{l}=O_{\psi_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}})
, we have conditional on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,1,b}=O_{\psi_{2},tc}(\sqrt{\log n}\max_{i}N_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}),\quad\Delta_{2,3,1,c}=O_{\psi_{2},tc}(\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}).

Putting together,

\Delta_{2,3,1}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,1}|\mathbf{E}]=O_{\psi_{2},tc}\left(\sqrt{\log n}\max_{i}N_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}\right).

Consider the
p
-th order term in the expansion of
\Delta_{2,3,2}
,

\delta_{p}=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{T_{i}-\pi_{k}}{\pi_{k}}g_{i}^{(p)}(1,\zeta_{i})\bigg(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\bigg).

Following conditional i.i.d argument as in Lemma
SA-14
, we can show condition on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\delta_{p}-\mathbb{E}[\delta_{p}|\mathbf{E},\bm{sgn}]

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2},tc}\left(\sqrt{\log n}\bigg(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}\max_{i}N_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-p\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}^{3}}{\min_{i}N_{i}^{4}}\bigg)\right).

Hence assuming
g_{i}(1,\cdot)
is
C^{p+1}
. Taylor expand
g_{i}(1,\cdot)
up to the
p
-th order, we get

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,3,2}-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2,3,2}|\mathbf{E},\bm{sgn}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\delta_{l}-\mathbb{E}[\delta_{l}|\mathbf{E},\bm{sgn}]+\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{D_{i}-v_{k}}{v_{k}}Y_{i}^{(p+1)}(1,\eta_{i}^{\ast})\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\zeta_{i}\right)^{p+1}+\text{error}

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{2},tc}\bigg(\sqrt{\log n}\bigg(n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}\max_{i}N_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\max_{i}N_{i}^{3}}{\min_{i}N_{i}^{4}}\bigg)\bigg).

I.3 Averaging Over
\mathbf{E}

From the previous steps, condition on
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\bigg\lVert\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n,UB}-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n,UB}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\bm{sgn}]-n^{-1}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}\bar{\mathbf{S}}_{l,i}(W_{i}-\pi_{l})\bigg\rVert_{2}=O_{\psi_{2}}(\bar{\mathtt{r}}_{n}),

where
\bar{\mathtt{r}}_{n}=\max_{k\in[K]}n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}+\sqrt{\log n}\max_{k\in[K]}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}(n\rho_{n})^{-1}+\sqrt{\log n}n^{-1/2}+(n\rho_{n})^{-(p+1)/2}
, and
\bar{\mathbf{S}}_{l,i}
is the vector
(\bar{S}_{1,l,i},\cdots,\bar{S}_{K,l,i})^{\mathbf{T}}
, where

\displaystyle\bar{S}_{k,l,i}=\frac{n}{n_{k}}\bigg[\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{k},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i,k}}(Y_{j}^{\prime}\left(1,\zeta_{j}\right)-Y_{j}^{\prime}\left(-1,\zeta_{j}\right))+\mathbbm{1}(k=l)(\mathtt{c}_{k}g_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})+\mathtt{d}_{k}\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})])\bigg],

with
\zeta_{i}=\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}N_{i,\ell}\pi_{\ell}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}N_{i,\ell}}
. Condition on
U_{i}
,
E_{i,j}
for all
1\leq j\leq n
are independent with
|E_{ij}|\leq 1
and
\mathbb{V}[E_{ij}|U_{i}]\lesssim\rho_{n}
. Hence using Bernstein’s inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}N_{i,\ell}\pi_{\ell}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}N_{i,\ell}}=\frac{\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}N_{i,\ell}\pi_{\ell}}{\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}N_{i,\ell}}=\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}n_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}g(U_{i})+O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}})}{g(U_{i})+O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}})}=\overline{\pi}+O_{\psi_{2}}((n\rho_{n})^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

with
\overline{\pi}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}p_{k}\pi_{k}
. The same argument as the proof for Lemma
SA-16
implies

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\bigg|\sum_{j\in\mathscr{C}_{k},j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{i}}(Y_{j}^{\prime}\left(1,\zeta_{j}\right)-Y_{j}^{\prime}\left(-1,\zeta_{j}\right))-p_{k}Q_{i}\bigg|=O_{\psi_{2},tc}((n\rho_{n})^{-1/2}),

with

\displaystyle Q_{i}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{G(U_{i},U_{j})}{\mathbb{E}[G(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j}]}(g_{j}^{\prime}(1,\overline{\pi})-g_{j}^{\prime}(-1,\overline{\pi}))\Big|U_{i},\bm{sgn}\Big].

Hence with
\mathbf{S}_{l,i}=(S_{1,l,i},\cdots,S_{K,l,i})^{\mathbf{T}}
, where

\displaystyle S_{k,l,i}=Q_{i}+\mathbbm{1}(k=l)p_{k}^{-1}(\mathtt{c}_{k}g_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})+\mathtt{d}_{k}\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\zeta_{i})]).

Hence by the same analysis as Equation (
SA-36
) in the proof of Lemma
SA-16
,

\displaystyle\bigg\lVert\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n,UB}-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n,UB}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\bm{sgn}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}\mathbf{S}_{l,i}(W_{i}-\pi_{l})\bigg\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\psi_{1},tc}((n\rho_{n})^{-1/2}\max_{k\in[K]}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{k},h_{k}}}+(n\rho_{n})^{-(p+1)/2}).

We already know
\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n,UB}
is the unbiased estimator. The same argument as Equation (
SA-53
) shows that condition on
\operatorname{sgn}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\bm{\tau}}_{n,UB}|\mathbf{E},\mathbf{g},\bm{sgn}]-\bm{\tau}_{n}\rVert_{2}=O(\exp(-n)).

This finishes the proof for the unbiased estimator.

II. The Hajek Estimator

The analysis will be the same as those for Lemma
SA-15
. For simplicity, denote
\widehat{\mathscr{p}}_{k}=n_{k}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}W_{i}
and
\mathscr{p}_{k}=\frac{1}{2}\tanh(\beta_{k}\mathscr{m}_{k}+h_{k})+\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{m}_{k}+\frac{1}{2}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{W_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{W_{i}Y_{i}}{\mathscr{p}_{k}}=\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{T_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}_{k}}\frac{\mathscr{p}_{k}-\widehat{\mathscr{p}}_{k}}{\mathscr{p}_{k}}.

The analysis in Lemma
SA-15
implies

\displaystyle 2(\widehat{\mathscr{p}}_{k}-\mathscr{p}_{k})=(1-\beta\sech^{2}(\beta\pi+h))(\mathscr{m}_{k}-\pi_{k})+O((\mathscr{m}_{k}-\pi_{k})^{2}),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})]=\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\pi)]+\mathbb{E}[g_{i}^{(1)}(1,\overline{\pi})(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\overline{\pi})]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[g_{i}^{(2)}(1,\pi^{\ast})(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\overline{\pi})^{2}]=O(\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-2\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}).

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{W_{i}Y_{i}}{\widehat{\mathscr{p}}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{k}}\frac{W_{i}Y_{i}}{\mathscr{p}_{k}}=-\frac{1-\beta_{k}(1-\pi_{k}^{2})}{1+\pi_{k}}\mathbb{E}[g_{i}(1,\overline{\pi})|\bm{sgn}]+O_{\psi_{1}}(\max_{1\leq l\leq K}n^{-\mathtt{r}_{\beta_{l},h_{l}}}).

The conclusion then follows from step
I. The Unbiased Estimator.

SA-12.7
Proof of Lemma
SA-25

We want to apply Lemma
SA-6
to the stochastic linearizations obtained from Lemma
SA-24
,

n_{l}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}(W_{i}-\pi_{l,(\mathbf{s})}),

for different blocks separately. First, we need to check if
\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}
satisfies the covariate constraints in Lemma
SA-6
. Recall
\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}=(S_{1,l,i,(\mathbf{s})},\cdots,S_{K,l,i,(\mathbf{s})})^{\mathbf{T}}
, where

\displaystyle S_{k,l,i,(\mathbf{s})}=Q_{i,(\mathbf{s})}+\mathbbm{1}(k=l)p_{k}^{-1}(R_{i,l,(\mathbf{s})}-\mathbb{E}[R_{i,l,(\mathbf{s})}]),\qquad 1\leq k,l\leq K,1\leq i\leq n,

Definitions of
Q_{i,(\mathbf{s})}
and
R_{i,l,(\mathbf{s})}
imply that
\min_{k,l\in[K]}\mathbb{E}[S_{k,l,i,(\mathbf{s})}^{2}]>0
and
\max_{k,l}|S_{k,l,i}|<\infty
almost surely, satisfying the conditions in Lemma
SA-6
. Hence

\displaystyle\sup_{A\in\mathscr{R}}|\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\beta},\bm{h}}(n_{l}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}

\displaystyle(W_{i}-\pi_{l,(\mathbf{s})})\in A|sgn(\mathscr{m}_{l})\mathbbm{1}(l\in\mathscr{L})=s_{l})-

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(n^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{l,(\mathbf{s})}^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{K}+\mathbbm{1}(l\in\mathscr{H}\cup\mathscr{L})n^{-1/2}\sigma_{l,(\mathbf{s})}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}]\mathsf{Z}_{(l)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n^{-1/4}\mathbbm{1}(l\in\mathscr{C})\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}]\mathsf{R}_{(l)}\in A)|=O\Big(\Big(\frac{\log(n)^{7}}{n}\Big)^{1/6}\Big),

where

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{l,(\mathbf{s})}=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}](1-\pi_{l,(\mathbf{s})}^{2}).

Now replacing
n_{l}
by
np_{l}
. The assumption that
n_{l}/n=p_{l}+O(n^{-1/2})
and the Nazarov inequality implies

\displaystyle\sup_{A\in\mathscr{R}}|\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\beta},\bm{h}}(n^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{C}_{l}}\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}

\displaystyle(W_{i}-\pi_{l,(\mathbf{s})})\in A|sgn(\mathscr{m}_{l})\mathbbm{1}(l\in\mathscr{L})=s_{l})-

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(n^{-1/2}p_{l}\bm{\Sigma}_{l,(\mathbf{s})}^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{K}+\mathbbm{1}(l\in\mathscr{H}\cup\mathscr{L})n^{-1/2}p_{l}\sigma_{l,(\mathbf{s})}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}]\mathsf{Z}_{(l)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n^{-1/4}\mathbbm{1}(l\in\mathscr{C})p_{l}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}]\mathsf{R}_{(l)}\in A)|=O\Big(\Big(\frac{\log(n)^{7}}{n}\Big)^{1/6}\Big),

(SA-54)

The independence between
\mathbf{S}_{l,i,(\mathbf{s})}
for different
i
’s and the independence between Ising-spins across blocks then imply the stochastic linearization from Lemma
SA-24
can be approximated by summation of right hand sides of Equation (
SA-54
). Lemma
SA-24
and Nazarov inequality applied on the
n^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathsf{Z}_{K}
part then imply the conclusion.
